# Added interfaces (2026-11-10): twisted-pair Ethernet generalizations (spec owner)

Source: `spec/interface-requirements.md` §1.14 (verdicts, WR-100TX-*), board B38/B39/B38a, model `scratch/ethernet/fe100.py`.
**100BASE-TX is admitted only through the general-purpose changes below.** None of them is an Ethernet block; each one also
serves other links and debug. **10BASE-T is not admitted.** A 3.3 V current-steering TX into a matched load reaches at most
1.39–1.45 V peak (2.2 V needs ≥ 116 mA or a dedicated voltage-mode driver). Owners: please answer each IX-9…IX-13 with accept,
reject or modify. A reject moves WR-100TX-* to the B38a board-PHY backup and needs no other change.

| ID | Change (generalization) | Owners | Spec / acceptance | Other uses (why it is general) | Area / power / risk | Req. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| IX-9 | **POST tap sign select + POST DAC ×1.2.** New static bit `wiredtx.config.post_polarity` (0 = inverted, de-emphasis, reset; 1 = same sign). Apply it as a P/N swap at the `CMD_POST = post & EN_POST` gate, implemented as an AO/mux gate with the **same gate type in the MAIN and TMDS CMD paths** so the main/post delay match is kept. POST DAC moves to `wtx_idac2m` (×1.2, 25.4 mA FS, as WT10 for MAIN). Pads: allow MAIN+POST with on-die TX termination off (external CT network, as TMDS/B9). Confirm the tail devices, metal and EM carry ≤ 51 mA on one leg for ≥ 100 µs, with the pad ≥ 1.7 V | wired_tx, pads (interlock, EM, headroom), digital (1 bit, stopped-only), integration (wired island VSS return +25 mA) | MAIN−POST delay skew ≤ 10 ps at 3.24 Gb/s unchanged versus today. Same-sign sum: \|I_MAIN+I_POST\| ≥ 46 mA at ss. Cancellation (I_MAIN = I_POST, toggling at 2 Gb/s): residue ≤ 1 % of peak after the B39 filter | (1+D) duobinary / PAM-3 TX at any rate. **2× TX amplitude for every low-rate mode** (OS ≥ 2: long-reach 125–834 Mb/s, link pulses, 100BASE-FX modules with high-swing inputs). Low-pass 2-tap FIR (EMI-limited links). MLT-3. Calibrated ½-step test levels for RX characterization | ≈ 3 gates per CMD leg (< 100 µm²); 0 static power; +4.4 mA only when POST is used at full scale. **Low risk** (static select outside the compact core). Low-risk alternative considered: an independent POST data stream (a second shift-register bank in `wtx_serq4`). Rejected because it touches the compact core, the highest-risk wired block | WR-100TX-TXLVL/-TXSHAPE/-ANEG |
| IX-10 | **Threshold DACs on the edge samplers only (e0–e3; d0–d3 untouched).** Each e-sampler gets an auxiliary offset pair (PAM-4/eye-monitor style StrongARM input), tail off when unused, selecting one of two signed 7-bit DACs VTH_A / VTH_B or off (per-sampler 2-bit select). Range ≥ ±250 mV referred to SL_P−SL_N, LSB ≤ 4 mV, monotonic, settling ≤ 50 ns to 1 LSB. Calibrated by the FPGA against the fine trim (INL ≤ 2 LSB after cal) | wired_rx (slicer, DAC), digital (registers + fast path IX-11), integration | Off state: added SL_P/N load ≤ 10 fF per sampler. Edge-decision timing shift ≤ 2 ps at 834 MHz quarter rate. Rerun the r4c extracted 834 MHz and CTLE-driven benches with the aux pairs present | **2-D eye scan / margining on every wired standard** (with `ptrim` and psel) in full/half-rate modes: debuggability-first. PAM-3/PAM-4/MLT-3 RX. DC restore / baseline-wander correction (common shift of A and B). Amplitude/squelch measurement. CTLE output-offset measurement | ≈ 0.02 mm² (4 aux pairs + 2 DACs); ≤ 0.6 mA when enabled, 0 off. **Medium-low risk**: it touches the freshly requalified slicer, so it is limited to the e-samplers with aux tails off by default. Low-risk alternative: wider coarse cap-imbalance trim (±150 mV) on e-samplers. Insufficient: static, PVT-nonlinear at large codes, no fast update ⇒ no BLW correction, and ±135 mV + BLW headroom is out of reach | WR-100TX-RXTH/-BLW/-SD |
| IX-11 | **Fast in-band threshold update.** An H2D opcode (as the RF fast-gain command) writes VTH_A/VTH_B (or a common offset added to both) with ≤ 300 ns command→DAC latency and ≥ 2 MHz update rate. D2H capture→pin latency fixed and readable | digital (transport, regfile) | Total FPGA DC-restore loop delay ≤ 0.5 µs (D2H + FPGA + H2D + DAC). Model residual: random data ≤ 5.6 % A, worst held level 13.5 % A (1 µs loop: 6.9 % / 26 %) | Any AC-coupled or transformer link with DC content (on-die AC coupling PKG-W1c, 8b/10b droop). Adaptive threshold servo for PAM. Fast eye-scan sweeps | RTL only, small. Low risk | WR-100TX-BLW |
| IX-12 | **Raw dual-lane full-rate capture.** `cfg_raw` with a forced psel register (`cdr.config.psel_force`, odd values so both lanes are e-samplers). Export the data word (psel) **and** the edge word (psel+4) to the host, 2 × ring rate | digital (pt_wrx_cdr_loop raw path, wired boundary, transport), wired_rx (capture: no change expected) | D2H 2 × 750 Mb/s = 1.5 Gb/s at ring 750 MHz (6× of 125 MBd). Both lanes word-aligned, with a fixed, known half-ring-cycle offset | FPGA oversampling CDR with two thresholds (IX-4 route promoted). Live edge statistics / jitter histograms on any standard. Eye-scan readout for IX-10 | RTL mux + register; low risk | WR-100TX-CDR/-HOST |
| IX-13 | **CTLE low-frequency zero bank.** 3-bit switched MIM bank (1.25 / 2.5 / 5 pF, floating between the two source nodes, series switches ≤ 150 Ω) across the existing degeneration ladder. At ladder code 7 (4.1 kΩ) the zero moves to 4.4–35 MHz with the same 15 dB boost. New field `ctle.config.lf_zero[2:0]` (reset 0 = today's response) | wired_rx (ctle), digital (3 bits, static), pads none | Code 0: GHz peaking change ≤ 0.5 dB and input/source parasitic ≤ 15 fF versus CTLE r2 extracted. Codes 1–7: monotonic zero 4.4–35 MHz at tt/ss/ff | **Long-reach low-rate links**: 100BASE-TX 0–100 m, long coax at ≤ 834 Mb/s (e.g. SD-SDI-class 270 Mb/s, 155 Mb/s), USB FS over long cables via the slicer. Equalizes board RC/AC-coupling droop at low rates. Lets the IX-4 low-rate mode cover real cables | ≈ 0.005–0.01 mm² (8.75 pF MIM), 0 power. **Low risk** (passive bank, off by default) | WR-100TX-RXEQ |

Free (no change): RX termination mode 10 with board CT bias (B38); `rxlevel` bypass/half; `pt_detect` energy codes; TX
quarter-rate 2 Gb/s 16× pattern (WT12) on a 500 MHz ring; RX ring at 750 MHz; 4B5B/scrambler/MLT-3/auto-negotiation in
the FPGA. The COB channel at ≤ 62.5 MHz is < 0.05 dB. 100BASE-FX (125 MBd NRZI to an optical module) is free on today's
hardware.
Totals if all are accepted: ≈ 0.03 mm², ≤ 0.6 mA die current (RX, when enabled). The TX tail current (40–51 mA) comes from the
board CT rail (B39) and returns through die VSS.

Contents: protection shell on `network-gc-d2` (PD23) plus, per leg, `pt_hs_leg`: segmented poly termination (10 base units on TERM_ENABLE + 15 trim units on TERM_TRIM0..3 = 1/2/4/8 units, 6 V switches to TERM_VCM), **on-die AC coupling** (MIM 4.9 pF, bottom plate on PAD) to RX, 40 kΩ RX→VBIAS (HPF 0.67–1.07 MHz), 6 V drain-diode clamps RX→VDD/VSS, and a 6 V drain diode VDD→ATB_ESD_RAIL tap that precharges the protection rail to 2.4–2.75 V (−0.12 pF/leg). Magic DRC 0, LVS unique, independent device audit 508/508, KLayout main 0 / antenna 0, LU audit 0 µm² beyond 15/20 µm. **Requalified (extracted, unchanged benches and limits):** ±100 mA 2.37–2.62 V; supply ramps 0 exceedances; unpowered 3.63 V / 50 Ω peer 0.15–0.34 µA; **HBM-like 2 kV 5.04–5.41 V, 0 of 404 MOS over their screen** (worst nfet_06v0 5.41 / 6.0 V); RX (CTLE gate) stays within −1.0…+1.0 V during HBM. Termination (VCM 0.3–0.5 V): code 0 64.8–116 Ω, code 15 24.4–38.7 Ω, 2–3.5 Ω/LSB around 50 Ω (tt 50 Ω ≈ code 5–6, ss ≈ 9, ff ≈ 2–3). **C per leg, trimmed to ≈ 50 Ω, CTLE 120 fF assumed: 1.11 (tt), 1.11 (ss32), 1.14 pF (ff22) ≤ 1.15 pF (IX-3); die-level diff RL at 2.4 GHz 8.5 / 8.4 / 8.0 dB.** With the COB chain (bond ±0.3 mm): RL 13.0–16.7 dB over 0.1–1.25 GHz, 5.7–6.5 dB over 1.25–2.5 GHz (PD26). RX path: MIM loss 0.27–0.33 dB, flat to 2.4 GHz; RX DC = VBIAS. Power-up (10 ns / 1 µs VDD ramp): no shunt trigger, VDD peak 70–80 µA / 5–8 µA, rail precharge t90 44–75 µs. Evidence `pd21-hspad-{safe-hbm,rterm,ac,rxpath,power}.json`. | wired RX (CTLE input), assembly, digital, board | **Wired RX:** connect the CTLE inputs to `RX_P/RX_N`; supply `VBIAS` = CTLE input CM (1.0 V; load is the 40 kΩ bias only, DC ≈ 0); drive `TERM_VCM` at 0.3–0.5 V (0.4 V nominal) from `pt_termination` (the RX CM no longer comes from the termination); rerun the CTLE/eye benches with `wired-receive-hs/physical.spice` and the COB chain. Keep the CTLE input C ≤ 120 fF/leg (budget used above); a USB/legacy mux at the CTLE input (PD22) must stay ≤ 20 fF on this node. **Assembly:** place `pad_hspad` on bidir[14]/[15] (PD22), mirrored like today's wirepad so the 56 µm overhang lies over the supply sites; route RX_P/RX_N ≤ 150 µm to the CTLE (M5, shielded, matched). **Digital:** PD27. |
| PD22 | **Pin plan / bond map (user decision), checked against the COB map (`spec/package-channel.json`).** The best wired pair on the board is bond 20/21 (contacts 60/59: next to GND contact 61, 0.4/0.8 mm from it, traces 5.34/5.20 mm, 0.8 ps intra-pair skew), so the **HS pair takes bond 20/21 (sites bidir[14]/[15], net names `WIRE_RX_N/WIRE_RX_P` kept for SATA/PCIe/DP/serial RX)** and **USB moves to bond 24/25 (bidir[18]/[19], contacts 56/55, 1.2/0.8 mm from GND, traces 3.18/5.41 mm → 13 ps skew, < 1 % of the 2083 ps USB HS UI): new nets `USB_DN`/`USB_DP` on the existing `pad_wirepad`**. Freed by folding: **WCLK_OBS** (bond 24) and **WDBG_OBS** (bond 25). **DBG_OBS1 (bond 40, north edge) is not needed for the pair and freeing it gains nothing for HS (wrong edge, ~3 mm from the wired block): recommend keeping it** (it is the only way to see two status bits at once); if the user still wants it folded, sel1 is a pure duplicate of sel0 and nothing is lost but simultaneity. **Observation mapping (no source lost):** (1) WCLK_OBS sources `cdr_recovered`, `wire_line`, `wire_sample`, `wclk_in` already exist on CLK_OBS (`pt_clk_obs16` codes 14, 2, 3, 7 via `pt_obs_pre4`, CO1/P3); observed f = f_src / 4 / 2^N instead of f_src / 2^N (full-rate observation of ≥ 500 MHz sources was never possible through the pad driver). (2) WDBG_OBS `wire_status[0..15]` → DBG_OBS0 `debug.dbg_obs.sel0` codes **37 + n** (6-bit field, max 52). (3) `block_observation` entries with path `wdbg_obs` (sel 3+n) become `dbg_obs` (sel 37+n); entries with path `wclk_obs` become `clk_obs` with the codes above. TX: no separate HS TX pair is possible without a 4th east pin (ESD_TEST is the TLP clamp site, WMON the only analog line monitor); TX stays on bond 28/29 (contacts 52/51, already next to GND contact 53), see PD24. Crosstalk: HS_RX↔WCLK_IN −23.5 dB @1.25 GHz (unchanged neighbour); USB↔WCLK_IN and USB↔WIRE_TX are new neighbours at USB rates (≤ 480 Mb/s). | digital (RTL/regfile/debug-controls.json/register-map), assembly (inventory.py CUSTOM_PADS/PROVIDER_SITES, pt_chip.sv, contract.json sites), board (B10 test points, connector pin labels), wired RX (USB HS receive path), spec owner (package-channel.json pin classes) | **Digital:** retire `debug.wclk_obs` and `debug.wdbg_obs` (and `wclk_obs*/wdbg_obs*` macro outputs), extend `dbg_src` with `wire_status[n]` at 37+n, sel0 maximum 52; remove `wire_line, wire_sample, wclk_in, cdr_recovered` from `debug.clk_obs.source.forbidden` once assembly binds clk_obs16 IN2/IN3/IN7/IN14 (CO1); update `register-map.md` and `block_observation` paths; keep DBG_OBS1 unless the user overrides. **Assembly/integration:** contract sites `WIRE_RX_P/N` stay on bidir[15]/[14] with `pad_hspad`; new `USB_DP/USB_DN` on bidir[19]/[18] with `pad_wirepad` (mirrored, overhang over bond 26/27 supply sites, as today at [14]/[15]); retire the `wclk_obs` 8-input `pt_clk_obs` instance and the two provider cells at bidir[18]/[19]. **Wired RX/USB:** USB HS receive previously used the shared serial receiver through wirepad RX_P/N (bypass/divider); either add a 2:1 mux at the CTLE input (≤ 20 fF on the HS node, 6 V device on the wirepad side) or a separate USB HS receiver at the wirepad; protocols that need the `rxlevel` divider also move to the USB pins. **Board:** B10 test points move (WCLK_OBS/WDBG_OBS gone; USB_DP/DN on connector 55/56, HS pair on 59/60). |
| PD23 | **HBM item (a): pad-side switches.** Analysis: a rail-referenced secondary can only hold a node at rail + 0.7 V = 4.6–5.3 V during 2 kV (rail 3.9–4.6 V), above the 3.63 V screen of every 3.3 V device, so **no 3.3 V device may have a terminal that follows the pad**; the conventional fix is 6 V oxide on every pad-tracking node **plus a primary clamp that keeps the pad under the 6.0 V screen**. Done: **`network-gc-d2`** (published): one extra 4-finger pad→rail diode row per leg (`protection_array.py --extra-up-rows 1`; rebuild DRC 0 / LVS match at array, network, net_ss and GC macro). HBM-like 2 kV pad peak 6.18–6.91 → **5.04–5.41 V** (tt27/ss0/ff85), C +0.14 pF/leg (+0.12 with precharge). Effect on the unchanged circuits rebuilt on d2 (`scratch/pads-pd21/q/*_d2-hbm.json`): **txpad 24–25 → 8 exceedances** (all cascodes, detect isolation now pass; the 8 left are the P-leg termination switches nfet_03v3 at 4.8–5.4 V); **wirepad 65–67 → 46** (all 6 V devices pass; 46 = 3.3 V termination/pull-down/divider-ground NMOS and the VDD-side high-side PMOS whose gate follows the floating well). **wirepad conversion** (`wired_receive.py pad_side_6v`, `stress_safe_compact_layout.py ... usbfix6v`; default reproduces the canonical netlist exactly): TERM ×8, DIFF_TERM ×5, DIV_GROUND, PULLDOWN, high-side SUP/RESTORE/FASTOFF/POWER → 6 V (USB-only after PD22, so TERM_VCM = 0 V and 6 V NMOS keep full drive; the pad-side PMOS keeps 3.3 V, its terminals stay within one junction drop). Built on d2: DRC 0 / LVS match, same 524 × 1513 µm outline. **HBM-like 2 kV: 0 exceedances (583 MOS fully observed, `q/wp7-hbm.json`)**; ±100 mA, ramps (ramp_slow 4 at exactly VDD = 3.63 V, as published), unpowered peer 0.3–0.5 µA clean (`q/wp6-family.json`). **Not published — functional regressions with the same device counts** (`q/wp6-usb.json` vs `pd13-usb-attachment.json`): USB HS termination 46 → 56 Ω (tt), 53 → 68 Ω (ss), 40 → 48 Ω (ff) (spec 45 Ω ± 10 %); FS rise 8–11 → 10–15 ns and fall up to 19.7 ns at ss (4–20 ns window, rise/fall matching lost); FS high level in the lifecycle fixture −0.12 V at ss/3.15 V (2.68 vs 2.80 V). Closing needs 16 (not 8) 6 V termination switches with a shorter poly and 2× the 6 V VDD-side PMOS, which grows the array and breaks the hard-coded receiver-discharge attach (`receive_pad_layout.py` 'changed access: VSS'); that re-route plus USB re-sizing is the next pads step, with the USB owner's FS slew/matching review. **txpad termination switches cannot simply become 6 V NMOS**: the TX output CM is 1.4–1.5 V (PD17), where a 6 V NMOS has ≈ 1.5 V overdrive at ss (≈ 510 Ω per 20 µm vs ≈ 60 Ω for 3.3 V), i.e. ≈ 6× width and ≈ +1 pF on the TX pad; see PD24. | pads (wirepad), wired TX, wired RX/USB, assembly | **Assembly:** future pad builds use `network-gc-d2` (same outline/ports as `network-gc`). **Wired RX/USB:** review FS rise/VOH of the 6 V high side in WP6 (USB FS 4–20 ns window). **Wired TX:** PD24 decision. |
| PD24 | **Low-C TX (proposal, not built).** A separate TX pair is not available (PD22), and the TX C (1.49 pF of driver devices) is the driver's own. Lowest-risk route to ≤ 1.15 pF and a clean HBM screen on bond 28/29: a **source-series-terminated (SST) voltage-mode driver merged with the PD21 termination units**: each unit = ppolyf_u + two 6 V NMOS (to a regulated VTX ≈ 0.6–1.0 V and to VSS), main/post split by unit count for de-emphasis, trim by unit count; no PMOS on the pad (no unpowered backfeed), every pad-reachable device 6 V with full gate drive (VTX ≤ 1 V), C ≈ the PD21 leg (≈ 1.0–1.1 pF/leg). Cost: a VTX regulator, a 3.3 V pre-driver into ≈ 1 pF of 6 V gate per phase, receiver-detect redone as a pulled-current test. The conventional alternative (CML with PMOS 6 V termination to VDD) backfeeds an unpowered VDD through the drain-well diodes (≈ 70 mA at a 3.63 V peer) unless the well is switched. TMDS (open-drain to the sink's 3.3 V) cannot share an SST/low-CM pad and would keep the present `txpad`. | wired TX (owner decision), pads (layout once decided), digital | Wired TX: choose SST (pads lays out the units on the PD21 shell) or keep `txpad` with the 8 residual HBM exceedances recorded as risk-accepted. |
| PD25 | **pt_pll_vreg area (item b).** 0.285 mm² per instance; device footprint 0.136 mm² (pass 0.060, gate cap 0.035, output cap 0.025, resistors 0.013), the rest is row/channel overhead. **(1) Regulate only the rings that need it:** the A3 supply-induced DJ (≈ 29 ps peak at 40–90 mV, 78 MHz) is 8.7 % UI at 3 Gb/s (pll_tx) and is outside the CDR loop bandwidth (pll_cdr), but 0.9 % UI on the 312.5 Mb/s host link (pll_host): **keep `pt_pll_vreg` on pll_tx and pll_cdr, put pll_host on the raw island rail (BYP strapped or no instance): −0.285 mm²** (0.86 → 0.57 mm²). **(2) Shrink each instance (proposal, not published):** halve the gate cap (30,000 → 15,000 µm²) and the output cap (20,000 → 10,000 µm²) and pack them into the resistor rows. Schematic, unchanged benches (`scratch/pads-pd21/vreg/`): PSRR worst 10–200 MHz −23.6 / −24.5 / −24.5 dB (tt/ss/ff; base −26.0 / −27.4 / −26.7; requirement ≥ 20 dB); ring at 78 MHz, 60 mV: Kvdd 13.3 / 14.7 MHz/V, DJ 2.60 / 2.87 ps (tt/ff; base 1.81 / 2.15), so ≤ 4.3 ps at the A2 worst 90 mV (< 5 ps). A further halving (10,000 µm² gate cap) drops PSRR to −21.8 dB (tt): not proposed. Layout-only check of the packed variant (convkit, Magic DRC 0, LVS unique): **398 × 606 µm = 0.241 mm²** (−15 %); the width is set by the 2 × 30 native pass units (6000 µm W at L 2.7 µm is needed for the 0.31–0.50 V dropout, so it does not shrink). **Total for the rings: 0.856 → 0.482 mm² (−44 %) with both steps**, −0.285 mm² with step 1 alone. Step 2 needs the extracted PSRR/ring/start-up reruns before publication. | assembly, wired RX (pll_ring), digital | Assembly: two instances (pll_tx, pll_cdr), 0.29 mm² each today (0.24 mm² after step 2); pll_host supply = the pll island rail directly. Digital: the pll_host BYP bit and its 2 ATB leaves are not needed. Wired RX: confirm the pll_host rate (312.5 Mb/s host link assumed) and its jitter budget. |
| PD26 | **COB return loss and board-side compensation (wired RX finding).** With the PD21 pad the low band meets PCIe/SATA (≥ 13 dB to 1.25 GHz, bond ±0.3 mm), but 1.25–2.5 GHz stays at 5.7–6.5 dB: the series path is ≈ 6.3 nH (bond 2.3 nH, the 86.6 Ω / 5.2 mm COB trace ≈ 2.7 nH-equivalent, via 0.36, connector 0.99), whose constant-k cutoff at 50 Ω is ≈ 2.5 GHz — a die-side fix cannot remove it (a die T-coil only cancels die C; with an ideal 50 Ω die the series L alone gives ≈ 5.8 dB at 2.4 GHz). **A single shunt C at the connector makes the high band worse.** A carrier-side **C1–L–C2 ladder** right at the connector (C1 0.9–1.05 pF, series 4–5 nH, C2 0.2–0.6 pF) lifts 1.25–2.5 GHz to 11–13 dB nominal; with ±10 % parts, R 45–55 Ω and bond ±0.3 mm it gives 6.0–8.4 dB with C1 ≤ 0.5 mm from the connector pads, 4.4–6.4 dB at 2 mm and 2.9–4.2 dB at 5 mm. So it helps (+2…+6 dB) only as a tightly placed, tight-tolerance (±0.05 pF) network; die C ≤ 1.0–1.15 pF is still worth ≈ 2 dB. Evidence `wired-receive-hs/pd21-rl-cob-and-board-compensation.json`. | board (spec/board-requirements.md), spec owner (package-channel §6 target), wired RX | Board: optional footprint pair per HS RX leg on the carrier at the connector (0201 C1, 0402 L ≈ 4.7 nH, 0201 C2), DNP by default; populate for SATA Gen2/PCIe 2.5 G RL compliance. Spec: keep "≥ 8 dB at 2.5 GHz" as a board+compensation target, not a die target. |
| PD27 | **To digital / pt_termination:** HS pair termination trim is **4 bits** (`TERM_TRIM0..3` = trim[k] AND term_en, 3.3 V), not the 3 bits of PKG-W1a; reset code 5 (≈ 53 Ω tt). TERM_VCM 0.3–0.5 V (0.4 V nominal) for the HS pair; USB pins (wirepad) TERM_VCM = 0 V. `RX_P/RX_N` are CTLE inputs behind the on-die MIM (no RX enable needed). `ATB_ESD_RAIL` of the HS pair is used as the rail-precharge input (its ATB leaf never existed); do not add one for this pad. | digital, wired RX (`pt_termination`) | `termination.config.trim[3:0]` per HS pair (reset 5); a `TERM_VCM` setpoint field or fixed 0.4 V in `pt_termination`. |

# Near-miss radio generalizations (spec owner, 2026-11-10)

Source: `spec/interface-requirements.md` §2.3 (verdict table, RF-HT40-*, RF-XLO-RANGE, RF-QVCO-RANGE, RF-SUBG-PROF,
RF-GNSS-RX, RF-DUPLEX, RF-RADAR, RF-BLE-CTE, RF-SDR-*, RF-SELFTEST, RF-PROF-SWITCH, RF-MULTICHIP), board B41–B45,
traced in `spec/performance-requirements.json` (14 rows). Each item generalizes something that already exists.
No item adds an RF block, an LO-path element or a pin. Owners: please answer accept / reject / modify. A reject
removes only the requirement rows named in that item.

| ID | Change (generalization) | Owners | Spec / acceptance | Other uses | Area / power / risk | Req. |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GX-1 | **`bbfilter` wide code.** New static bit `consolidation.bbfilter.wide` (I and Q together). Halve the base integrator C (split the base unit, one TG on the switched half), or parallel the unit R, in all three stages. Reset 0 gives today's response. | rf_rx (bbfilter), digital (1 bit) | Widest corner 20–30 MHz at every envelope corner after Q-trim (today 11.3–21.2 MHz). Peaking ≤ 1 dB with `qtrim`. Droop ≤ 3 dB at 19.1 MHz. RX and TX directions. | HT20 margin at the slow corner (11.3 vs 10 MHz). SDR span ≥ 36 MHz. GNSS L5 full lobe. HT40 TX reconstruction | ≈ 24 TGs, < 0.002 mm², 0 mA. Risk: OTA GBW 270 MHz / 30 MHz ≈ 9 → peaking (Q-trim covers it); the switch adds parasitic C only on the switched half | RF-HT40-RX/-TX, RF-SDR-CAPTURE |
| GX-2 | **Host-fed 80 MS/s TX.** `digital.transport_config.tx_interp` gets a 3rd value `external`: rf_tx_clk = 80 MHz, `pt_interp2x` forwards host sample pairs (even/odd) instead of computing the halfband. Mux only. | digital (pt_interp2x, transport RF TX side), FPGA | H2D carries 80 MS/s × 2 × 12 b = 1.92 Gb/s, so the **full-rate host link** is needed in this mode (an exception to PWR-HOST-RF div2). DAC 80 MS/s is already demonstrated | Any TX wider than ≈ ±8 MHz with a clean ±21 MHz region: HT40, wideband test signals, digital FMCW chirps | ≈ 20 cells. Host IO current at full rate (hostio budget) in this mode only | RF-HT40-TX |
| GX-3 | **Duplex configurations accepted.** The commit validator and WarmHop accept, inside the RF engine: (a) `bbfilter.direction=rx` + `tx_dac_direct=1` + rfdriver unmuted + LNA/rxmix on; (b) `direction=tx` + `rx_direct=1`. Both converters run, and both host directions carry I/Q at the same time. Today WarmHop rejects "a direction change disguised as a hop", and the duplex combination is not listed as a supported combination. | digital (validator, warm hop, transport), FPGA | RTL test of both combinations: commit accepted, both streams gap-free | IX-7 leakage loopback (it already needs TX and RX on), RF-SELFTEST, radar, CS calibration | No hardware. Power is already booked for both RF directions warm | RF-DUPLEX, RF-SELFTEST, RF-RADAR |
| GX-4 | **Epoch-switched profile.** `consolidation.bbfilter.bw`, `.wide`, `.rx_direct`, `.tx_dac_direct` and the gain fields become updatable at the warm-hop epoch (a new update class next to stopped/live), so one hop applies LO + filter + gain together. Sample rate and host clock stay stopped-only. | digital (regfile generator, warm hop), rf_rx (bw switching transient) | Profile switch ≤ 50 µs. Filter settled to 1 dB / 2° inside the hop (bbfilter direction settles in 0.15 µs extracted; bw step not yet run) | Time-sliced BLE ↔ Wi-Fi ↔ 15.4 coexistence; spectrum scan; CTE filter code | ≈ 30 FF shadow; no analog change | RF-PROF-SWITCH, RF-SDR-SCAN |
| GX-5 | **Off-band clocking and spur plan.** With an external LO outside 2.4–2.5 GHz the converter clock comes from `host_follow` (CS_HF), not an LO division, and is documented as such in block-diagram "Warm RF hopping" ("converter sampling remains LO-derived" becomes a per-mode choice). register-map "Macro clock plan" gains per-band rows: 902–928 MHz (the 40 MHz sample harmonic at 920 MHz is inside the band; mark channels within ±1 MHz), GNSS L5 (78.125 × 15 = 1171.875 MHz is 4.6 MHz from L5, so use the 62.5 MHz core), L1/L2 and 2.2–2.8 GHz. | digital (clock plan doc, clockmux select), spec, FPGA | Spur map per band: no core or sample harmonic within ±1 MHz of a used channel, or within a GNSS main lobe ± 1 MHz | Every off-band use | None (documentation + an existing mux setting) | RF-XLO-RANGE, RF-GNSS-RX |
| GX-6 | **QVCO loop over the whole tank.** When the frequency loop (LO3) is built, its divider/fraction range and `rfpll.config.vco_coarse` band selection cover the full tank (≥ 2.1–2.9 GHz input to the prescaler; TSPC divides to 3.5 GHz tt) with no 2.4–2.5 GHz clamp in hardware or the validator. | timing (lcvco loop), digital (validator ranges) | Band sweep sim: lock across the per-die tank range; 2.40–2.50 GHz at all corners | 13 cm amateur, S-band, LTE B40/B41 monitoring (RX), per-die characterization | 0 (range choice only) | RF-QVCO-RANGE |
| GX-7 | **Deterministic multi-die sync.** Specify and test `clocktree.config.sync_arm` / `sync_epoch`: the sample epoch starts a fixed, readable number of sample clocks after a common H2D sync event, identical on every die (0-sample spread). | digital (clocktree sync, IX-8 latency register) | RTL test with two macro instances on skewed H2D clocks within HL-H2D-MARGIN | 2×2 MIMO, true-array AoA, coherent capture across dies | 0 | RF-MULTICHIP |
| GX-8 | **RF_TX hot-switch tolerance (analysis only).** Simulate rfdriver code 15 into an open and a short load for 100 ns (antenna switch during TX, AoD CTE, B33 T/R switching) with the 6 V cascode and pad clamp: no device over its rating, no latch, recovery ≤ 1 µs. | rf_tx, pads | One transient sim per load | T/R switch faults, AoD | none | RF-BLE-CTE |
| GX-9 | **TX→RX isolation in duplex (analysis only).** RF_TX → RF_RX coupling including the COB chain (package-channel.json pin pair), the substrate and the shared LO lines (RF_TX → AUX_CLK_N is −17.5 dB in the COB model), with both chains on. Target ≥ 30 dB at 2.45 GHz. | rf_rx, rf_tx, pads, package | One S-parameter / transient run | Every TDD mode (mute leakage), RF-TX-MUTE | none | RF-DUPLEX |

**Not admitted (c):** on-chip LO ÷2/÷3 sub-band modes (these need a quadrature divider and a mux in the most fragile path,
and ÷3 gives no quadrature; the external LO covers these bands for free); 5 GHz harmonic or sub-harmonic mixing (the COB
series ≈ 6 nH path gives a low-pass cutoff ≈ 2.5–3.5 GHz, the 3rd LO harmonic is −9.5 dB with ×3 I/Q skew, and the
6 V driver has no gain at 5.5 GHz); ACR-compliant HT40 RX (it needs a ≥ 60 MS/s ADC, and 45 MS/s already fails at
ss). **Free (a), no change:** 2.2–2.8 GHz and 0.6–1.7 GHz with an external LO (board B42/B44), BLE CTE AoA/AoD (B41,
FPGA GPIO), GNSS RX, the loopback BIST recipe, and RSSI scanning.
Totals if all are accepted: < 0.005 mm², 0 mA standing current, no new pins; one exception to host div2 in HT40 TX mode.

# Host-link bandwidth (HB-1..HB-9), host-link review, 2026-11-10

Source: `scratch/reviews/host-link-bandwidth.md`. Spec: `spec/interface-requirements.md` §3.3 (HL-RATE, HL-H2D-PADIN, new HL-DIR / HL-FRAME / HL-MARGIN / HL-RATE-DIR / HL-STRETCH, WR-HL-LINECODE), `spec/board-requirements.md` HL-B1/B2/B7 and ECP5.
Goal: 3.0 / 3.125 / 3.24 Gb/s streaming (raw RX, 8b/10b both ways, full duplex) and HT40 TX fit with ≥ 4 % margin. No new pads, no pin-rate increase, no custom IO.
**Digital AC-1 owner:** sequence HB-2/HB-6 after the AC-1 `pt_quad_link` work lands. None of these blocks AC-1.

| ID | Change | Affects | Action requested |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| HB-1 | **D2H full-rate electricals (needed today).** The D2H cell is `bi_t` (`pt_chip.sv`), not `bi_24t` (AC-1's 177.9 MHz pad evidence). At ss/32 °C/3.15 V, 6 pF + COB + 27 Ω, PRBS7, the eye at 156.25 MHz is: `bi_t` slow slew **1.65 ns vs 1.68 ns needed (fails)**, fast 2.29 ns. Fast unstaggered = 486 mV > HL-SSO 300 mV. `hostio.controls.d2h_stagger` is a register **with no RTL consumer**. | digital, pads/assembly | **Digital:** implement `d2h_stagger`: odd D2H lanes (and the clock with the even group) launched through a fixed 0.4–0.8 ns delay chain. Use **not** ½ UI. Validator: full rate requires `slew=0` (fast), `drive=3`, `d2h_stagger=1`; slow slew only at div2/div4. Apply the same to the north bank when HB-6 lands. |
| HB-2 | **Frame length L ∈ {64, 256}** (new stopped-only field `digital.transport_config.frame_len`). L=256: header words 0–4 unchanged, payload 5–255 (251/256 = 0.980). Under exclusive ownership the count is the 12-bit `{iq[5:0],wc[5:0]}`; sequence/opcode/argument are unchanged. | digital (`pt_quad_link`, `pt_dense_header`, validator, regmap), FPGA reference design | Quad counter 16→64 per frame; count remap; validator rejects L=256 together with fast commands (RF AGC/opcode4, 100BASE-TX threshold updates, USB direct bytes) and with non-exclusive owners. ≈ 25 FF. Reference model/codec: parametrize L. |
| HB-3 | **8b/10b offload stays MUST in both directions** (`pt_lc_rx`/`pt_lc_tx`). It is required for full-duplex 3.0–3.24 Gb/s. | digital | Keep. Confirm `pt_lc_tx` with L=256 packing residuals across frames. |
| HB-4 | **Independent D2H / H2D rate.** New field `debug.host_link.d2h_rate` (full/div2/div4), separate from the H2D side (H2D is already captured through the CDC FIFO on the FPGA clock). | digital (rate clock, validator), power | Allow H2D full with D2H div2/div4 (HT40 TX 12-bit) and D2H full with H2D div2 (ECP5 hosts). The core runs at the faster side's word/4. ≈ 10 FF. Power owner: restate PWR-HOST-RF as a D2H-rate rule. |
| HB-5 | **Hold-padding.** Unadvertised payload slots repeat the previous word (today: padding pattern). | digital, FPGA ref | ≈ 0 FF. It cuts D2H toggles by ≈ 45 % at 54 % load, so hostio/SSN scale with payload, not lane count. |
| HB-6 | **RX2: reversible north bank.** `hostio.lane_dir` (stopped-only, reset BAL=0; 1=RX2; 2=TX2 if HB-8). In RX2, H2D_CLK + H2D_D[9:0] (`bi_24t`, already bidirectional) become a second forwarded D2H group. **Whole bank, clock included; never per lane.** The payload pairs as a **lockstep twin**: one frame schedule, each payload beat carries 2 quads (south = even, north = odd); the header is replicated per bank with a bank-ID bit in the argument; the egress FIFO is written in 80-bit pairs. RX2 has no H2D; control is on SPI. | digital (north 4:1 gearbox + DDR launch at the north pad edge, twin framer, egress pair write, release-check FSM, regmap), assembly (`pt_chip.sv` / `pt_host_physical`: north A/OE/SL/PU/PD from the macro instead of ties; one clock-tree branch carrying the chip launch clock to the north gearbox), board (HL-B1/B2/B7) | ≈ 330 FF, +14 macro pins, **no pad changes**. Safety: north OE from an async-reset flop ANDed with link enable (0 at reset/power-up). Before OE=1 run the **release check**: PD on → all north Y=0 within 10 µs, then PU on → all Y=1. On failure set sticky `dir_contention` and keep OE=0. Reverse: OE off first. Training pattern on both groups. Result: raw wired RX 3.0/3.125/3.24 Gb/s at 52/54/56 % of 5.76 Gb/s. |
| HB-7 | **North-bank SSN.** In RX2 the north bank has 11 outputs on `VDD/VSS_HOST_B`. | package/power (PKG-08), pads | Give the north return-set inductance and the bounce (fast slew, stagger on/off) in the same model as the D2H 0.8 nH / 486 mV. It must meet HL-SSO ≤ 300 mV with stagger. |
| HB-8 | *Optional* **TX2: reversible south bank** (D2H_CLK + D2H_D become an H2D group captured on the D2H_CLK pin). Flow control is an XOFF watermark on a new `dbg_obs` select; status on SPI. | digital (≈ 650 FF: south 1:4 + CDC FIFO, twin deframer), assembly | Build only if the FF budget allows. No listed mode needs it, given HB-2/HB-3 and MODE8. It enables raw TX playback above 2.9 Gb/s and 12-bit HT40 TX at div2 (67 %). |
| HB-9 | **Stretch (non-gating):** a host-chosen 175 MHz pin clock (3.43 Gb/s/direction at L=256). | digital STA | Report the transport core slack at 87.5 MHz alongside 78.125 MHz. No hardware change. 200/250 MHz are not supported: H2D input 191.6 MHz at ss_125C, and `bi_t` fast has a 0 ps margin at 200 MHz. |

Also, for the spec owner/RF: HT40 TX's default configuration becomes **BAL + H2D div2 + MODE8** (1.28 of 1.44 Gb/s, 89 %; 8-bit SQNR ≈ 38 dB after crest ≫ 19 dB EVM). This removes the GX-2 full-rate exception. **Converters/digital:** confirm the GX-2 `external` 80 MS/s path accepts MODE8 samples.
Rejected (reasons in the review §3/§6):
- folding DBG_OBS1 or SPI into host data;
- idle/ordered-set deletion as a capacity fix;
- 200–250 MHz DDR;
- custom LVDS host pads;
- WIRE_TX → FPGA GTP uplink.

# FPGA connection plan (FPGA-1..FPGA-5) and spec cleanups, spec owner, 2026-11-11

Source: `spec/fpga-connection-plan.md`. Spec edits made: WR-HL-LINECODE streamable cap; new HL-MARK (IX-8 reworded); PWR-HOST-RF full-rate exception; HL-B8, B6 and B10 (DBG_OBS0/1 and CLK_OBS to FPGA pins).

| ID | Change | Affects | Action requested |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| FPGA-1 | **D2H training hold.** The 8-word prefix is sent once per link start, so FPGA per-bit delay training must restart the link for each tap step (32–128 restarts). HB-5 hold-padding also leaves few edges during payload. | digital (`pt_quad_link_tx`), regmap | Recommended: a stopped-only `train_hold` control that repeats T0/T1 on every enabled D2H group (both banks in RX2) until RUN. ≈ 2–4 FF. |
| FPGA-2 | **RX2 host clock source.** In RX2 `H2D_CLK` is an output, so `host_follow` has no input clock. | regfile validator | Reject `hostio.lane_dir`=RX2 together with `debug.clk_select.host_clk`=host_follow or `clocktree.config.wire_host_source`=host_follow. Do the same for TX2 if it is built. |
| FPGA-3 | **HB-4 is required for ECP5 hosts.** ECP5 LVCMOS33 outputs are ≤ 150 MHz, so H2D must run at div2 while D2H runs at full rate. Without HB-4, ECP5 hosts are div2 in both directions. HB-4 is also the precondition of the PWR-HOST-RF GX-2 exception. | digital | Treat HB-4 as required, not optional. |
| FPGA-4 | **Residual contention after the RX2 release check.** Nothing on chip detects an FPGA that drives a reversed bank after OE=1 (≈ 30–40 mA per pin, continuous). That makes the gateware a de facto safety layer. | coordinator, digital, pads | Decide: accept it as a protocol violation (HL-B7), or add a cheap detection. |
| FPGA-5 | **IX-8 reworded (HL-MARK).** The chip marks TX launch and fast-control apply in the D2H stream (reason code plus ingress quad pointer, `pt_gain_rx` mark path, ≈ +15 FF) and publishes a fixed per-mode latency. The FPGA owns the 64-bit sample counters. No on-chip timestamp counter. | digital (S5), RF | Build S5 as the event mark (T20 already lists it). |
| T21 | **Hardening AC1_R4 (post-route, 1716 × 1455 µm; `scratch/digital-hardening-ac1-r4`).** Physical clean: route DRC 0, KLayout main + antenna 0, Magic DRC 0, LVS clean (the AC1_R2 "4 LVS errors" were `pt_lc_tx` registers `U`/`u`, which collide in case-insensitive netgen; renamed). Std-cell area 1.503 mm², 62 % utilization, 63,413 cells. **Timing is NOT closed.** Characterized ss_032C_3v15 setup slack (data paths): egress_wtx −15.6, hcore −14.4 (rate1) / −1.6 (div2), wrx −12.0, wtx −8.1, egress_rftx −7.9, ingress_wrx −7.7, hddr −7.7 (D2H clock-pin output budget), rftx −4.6, h2d −4.5, hword −2.5, rfrx −1.3, ingress_rfrx +0.02, fc* +0.49. Outside the transport: ref −26.5 (fcb snap → cdr_freq_locked), sclk −17.1 (regfile commit_count), ctw −8.7, ctdet −4.2. Hold ≥ +0.05 ns at ss; **ff22/ff32 hold wtx −0.34 ns**: `wire_tx_data[0..19]` from the PRBS generator's rising-edge word bypasses the falling-edge D0..D19 launch flops (T2 contract); the fix is S3 (pattern generator behind the lane's wire_low/high flops). The pre-route screen under-predicted post-route by about 2×. Root causes: synchronizer/active nets used as data with large fanout (egress `rd_active` in the playback-empty mux, a T13 regression; the `e2` enable into `pt_lc_rx` clears), deep pop decisions (framer `take`, `pt_lc_tx` pop) into FIFO flag registers, and the write-enable fanout to 1,120 array flops. | digital (both agents), flow owner | Next pass: register the playback empty (no `rd_ready` in data), replicate/pipeline enables, registered pop (skid) on both banks, ingress write through a registered per-entry enable; the regfile `commit_count` path and the fcb lock path belong to the other digital agent. |
| T22 | **Queue added (coordinator, 2026-11-11):** HB-2 L=256 frames, HB-4 per-direction rate (now required, FPGA-3), HB-5 hold-padding, HB-6 RX2 north-bank reversal with FPGA-4 contention detector (pad readback ≠ driven value for N cycles ⇒ tri-state the bank + sticky status; no reliance on gateware), FPGA-1 training-hold (repeat the 8-word prefix until released, 2–4 FF), FPGA-2 validator rejects RX2 + follow-host-clock, FPGA-5 IX-8 HL-MARK (chip marks + fixed latencies; FPGA counters; the same mark path as the offload audit's S5). | digital transport, regfile owner | Sequenced after AC-1 core timing closes. |

## HL-SCALE (user-approved 2026-11-11): graceful degradation on weak FPGAs. Owner: digital, then spec
- (a) The host clock works at any frequency from 156.25 MHz down to ~10 MHz, with no minimum-frequency hazard; a lower clock only reduces throughput.
- (b) Lane count is selectable per direction (20/10/5/2/1 D2H, matching H2D).
- (c) Over-capacity configurations are rejected by the validator or flagged by a sticky overflow bit. They never corrupt data silently.
- (d) Register access and debug always work at 1 lane with a slow clock.
- Spec follow-up: add an HL-SCALE row and a capability-vs-link-config table to spec/interface-requirements.md and spec/fpga-connection-plan.md.
- HL-SCALE (c) revised (user 2026-11-11): explicit opt-in LIMP MODE (register bit). Over capacity, TX repeats an on-chip pattern buffer at full line rate, and excess RX is dropped with a sticky drop counter and gap markers. Without the bit, the validator still rejects the configuration. Reuse pt_pattern and the existing stream mux.
| LO10 | **LO round 3 (LO owner, 2026-11-11): queue review.** r3 QVCO all-corner guaranteed range is **2.33–2.53 GHz** (r2 said 2.38–2.615); span 1.93–2.98 GHz. RF-QVCO-RANGE row basis needs updating. The ff "drive fail" was a criterion artifact (CM 2.14–2.19 V vs 2.1 V edge; extracted chain passes at ff band 0). **Systematic I/Q error at the LO pins through extracted `pt_lo_ext`: −92.5 to −93.7° (2.5–3.7°) on all 5 chain points; RF-LO-JIT skew ≤ 1.0° fails without D-LO-2.** | spec owner (RF-QVCO-RANGE, RF-LO-JIT basis) | Update rows; evidence `analog/timing/lcvco/results.json`, README "round 3". |
| LO11 | **D-LO-1 counter-proposal (quantified).** A 50 Ω per-leg shunt on the passive external path needs 8–10 Vpp open-circuit (+18–20 dBm/leg) to reach on ≥ 0.9 V at ss, pads then swing 3.2 V / −0.7 V (clamp and ESD diode): usable range < 2 dB, not ≥ 10 dB. Proposal: feed the external LO through the existing `pt_lo_cml` limiter (pads → AC-couple → LOI_*), keep 100/200 Ω termination only for return loss. Amplitude detector on the CML VSN node via ATB (as LO9). | package-channel owner (PKG-05), pads, digital (mode bit), board (B3 window republished after the bench) | Accept / reject; LO owner then benches the CML ext path through the COB chain. Evidence `scratch/lo/dlo1/results.json`. |
| LO12 | **D-LO-2 implementation.** `pt_lo_iqtrim`: n-well varactor bank on each LO_* node, LSB 4 µm², **5-bit magnitude + sign** (simulated 4-bit: 0.67–0.82°/LSB, ±10–12° at tt/ss/ff; on-level cost ≤ 55 mV). Needs 6 register bits `rfpll.config.lo_iq_trim[5:0]` (sign-magnitude), reset 0; one well-drive inverter per bit per pair. Amplitude trim ±1 dB still open (proposal: 2-bit CML tail trim per path). | digital (regfile bits + validator), integration (LO bundle area ≈ 4 × 124 µm² varactor) | Add bits; LO owner lays out with `pt_lo_ext`. |
| LO13 | **Frequency loop (LO3/GX-6/IX-5) architecture.** 50 MHz XO on `REF_IN` (internal-LO mode; B23 50.625 MHz OK), PFD 50 MHz, extended 2/3 MMD N = 32–127, MASH 1-1-1 16-bit fraction + 4-step band-search FSM in std cells at 50 MHz, CP 100 µA, R 22.1 k / C2 67 pF / C1 5.2 pF, fc 0.4 MHz PM 60°. Behavioural: 78 MHz CS hop phase-settled (≤ 2°) in ≤ 18.3 µs incl. band search (IX-5 limit 75 µs); range 1.75 GHz–MMD limit covers the whole tank (GX-6). PN budget meets RF-RX-LOPN with ≥ 5.2 dB, but integrated 1.35 ps rms vs RF-LO-JIT 1.05 ps. | digital (ΣΔ, band-search FSM, `rfpll.config` N.frac and band fields without ISM clamp), board (B23 XO frequency note), timing (MMD, PFD/CP, filter: transistor level not started) | Digital confirms fields; LO owner builds transistor-level MMD/CP. Evidence `analog/timing/lcvco/loop_design.json`. |
| T23 | **HL-SCALE (weak FPGA), status and cost.** (a) **Supported, 0 FF.** Every host-side flop runs on the host clock or its /2 (hddr, hcore), with Gray CDC FIFOs to the line and converter clocks. No timer counts host-clock cycles against a minimum frequency, so any `host_word_clk` ≤ 156.25 MHz (and `rate` /2 /4 below that) only lowers capacity. Benches cover 156.25, /2 and /4; a 10 MHz bench is not run yet. (b) **Not built.** Proposal: per-direction lane width {10, 5, 2, 1} (20 = HB-6 RX2). A width converter between the 4:1 gearbox and the pins (40-bit quad → n lanes, 4/8/20/40 beats per quad), with a quad-enable to the core instead of a new clock; unused lanes get OE off. Capacity at 156.25 MHz is 2.88 / 1.44 / 0.58 / 0.29 Gb/s. Cost ≈ 110 FF (2 × 40-bit shifters + beat counters + selects), ≈ 0.01 mm². It touches the framer (stall input), so it comes after AC-1 timing. (c) **Partly.** Overflow never corrupts stored data: bank writes are gated by the registered full, and the sticky ingress/egress/H2D overflow and `rx_fault` status bits report it. Missing: the D2H gap is not marked in the stream, and the validator has no capacity rule. Needed: a validator rule "payload rate ≤ capacity(rate, lanes, L)" (regfile owner, ≈ 0 FF; it needs a payload-rate field for RF), plus the T24 drop counter and mark. (d) **Supported.** SPI/regfile is independent of the host link. MEMORY v3 capture/playback works SPI-only with no host clock (`tb_memory_mode`, host_clk = 0). DBG_OBS/CLK_OBS are SPI-configured. | regfile owner (validator rule, lane fields), FPGA, pads (per-lane OE) | Regfile: add `hostio.lane_count_d2h/h2d` (stopped-only) and the capacity rule. |
| T24 | **Limp mode (user revision of HL-SCALE (c); not yet built, cost estimate).** Opt-in stopped-only bit (proposed `debug.host_link.limp`); never entered implicitly. Without it, the validator rejects over-capacity configurations. **TX:** reuse the existing stream muxes: limp forces the pattern-generator select (`prbs_select`, any `debug.pattern.tx_mode`, incl. the user word), or MEMORY v3 looped playback (the egress bank as a 24-quad = 960-bit user pattern buffer) at full line rate; ≈ 2 gates. **RX:** pushes that find the ingress bank full are dropped (already true, no corruption) and counted in a 16-bit saturating sticky `dropped_quads` counter (Gray-coded for SPI, ≈ 16 FF + 16 regfile sync FF). The first quad after a drop run is marked through the existing gain mark path (`mark_ptr`/`mark_toggle` → status frame) with reason code "gap" and the dropped count, so the FPGA sees each gap's position. This is the same event-mark mechanism as S5/FPGA-5 (≈ 15 FF, shared). Total ≈ 50–60 FF, ≈ 0.005 mm², no new datapath. | regfile owner (`limp` field, `dropped_quads` status, validator exemption), FPGA (gap-frame parsing) | Regfile: define the two fields; transport implements after AC-1 timing. |
| RFT-1 | **DAC antenna ×3 fixed (scratch, not published).** The 3 ANT.16_ii_ANT.4 hits are ONE net, `HOLD_ZERO` (block M3 151 µm + `dac_logic` internal M3 233 µm → 5 V gate 0.898 µm², ratio 464). The K1 planner judged the block wire alone (ratio 182 < 200) because it cannot see macro-internal routing. Side effect: the next adc_i/q regeneration may add diodes on `adc_sar_logic` input nets (more conservative). |
| RFT-2 | **rfdriver reset-state leak fixed, rf_tx evidence regenerated.** The RC-3 follow-up `P3C_*` pull-downs fought the VDD-fed source followers (pre-amp loads hold p2 at VDD when off), drawing **2.0–2.4 mA static in reset**, so rf_tx `safe` FAILED family-wide. P3C is needed: without it, the rail-on 100 mA pad pulse puts 4.0 V on the TS/SF gates (`scratch/rf-tx/stress_nop3c`). Fix: the SF drains get their own switched header `sfd` (`SFH_a/b`, 2 × 640 µm PMOS, gate `pre_off`). Results: reset 7.8 µA (ext, 3.63 V); stress pulse_on tt/ss 0 exceedances; layout DRC 0, netgen unique, KLayout 0/0, **135,222 µm²** (was 133,269). Duplex: 262.9 / 332.0 / 231.8 (+8.2). `--check` now FAILS on the rf_tx and duplex allocations. | spec (power), island_support, pads | (a) Spec: raise the rfdriver allocation in analog-architecture.json to ~59 mA, and decide whether the RX blocks (ADC 16, rxfilter 7.5, LNA 2.85 mA…) really stay on in TX-only mode. The output-stage current (~36 mA at code 15) comes from the board feed via the RF_TX chokes; confirm the board feed is NOT taken from the switched island rail. Otherwise the switch sees ~60 mA+. |
| RFT-4 | **GX-8 done (analysis): RF_TX hot-switch.** rfdriver (6 V cascode schematic, pre-RFT-2 SF header; on-state unaffected) + the pads' extracted rf-tx macro + 100 nH feed chokes. Code 15, saturated drive, load hot-switched to OPEN or SHORT for 100 ns (`scratch/rf-tx/gx8.py`, gx8.json). tt/27/3.3: 0 gate / 0 DS exceedances. Pad peak 4.07 V (open) / 3.97 V (short). Swing during the fault +10 % (open) / collapses (short). Rail current 59.3 → peak 60.7 mA; **recovery < 10 ns (≤ 1 µs required)**, no latch. Safety corners ff/0/3.63 and ss/85/3.63: the same 115 / 91 devices are 0–1.8 % over the 3.63 V screen (SF/TS gate-bulk ≈ 3.70 V) **before, during and after** the fault, i.e. a normal-operation item at 3.63 V, not caused by the hot switch. | rf_tx, pads | Pads/spec: decide whether ≤ 1.8 % over 3.63 V on SF gates during RF-on at a 3.63 V rail is acceptable (screen = abs max). |
| RFT-5 | **GX-9 done (analysis): TX→RX isolation, duplex, 2.45 GHz** (`scratch/rf-tx/gx9.py`). Inputs: the COB lumped chains for RF_TX/RF_RX/AUX_CLK (channel_model.py), mutual bond L between every pin pair (k 0.007–0.011 TX↔RX) and connector (k ≤ 0.004), the driver's extracted output admittance, a substrate net (junction C to sub, R_sub, taps) and P/N mismatch. **Isolation 78 dB balanced, 66 dB with 20 % P/N mismatch, 48.8 dB worst (weak taps 300 Ω, R_sub 100 Ω, 2× junction C, 20 % mismatch): ≥ 30 dB met.** Second finding: RF_TX → AUX_CLK_N on-die is **−28 dB** per pin (−34 dB differential). With TX at +5 dBm that puts −23 dBm on an external-LO input. | LO / timing owner, rf_rx | LO: check that −23 dBm of TX carrier on AUX_CLK (external-LO route) is acceptable (pulling, LO phase error in duplex); otherwise swap AUX_CLK away from pad 61 or add a guard ground bond. Analytic, not EM; on-die routing (rfdriver↔LNA) depends on the v4 floorplan and is not included. |
| T25 | **Digital macro timing closure (2026-11-11, digital AC-1 timing agent): cross-block notes.** (1) **D2H is now constrained source-synchronously** (integration/pt_digital_macro.sdc): at full rate each `d2h_pin` must change within ±0.64 ns (0.2 UI) of the `d2h_clk_pin` edge it belongs to, at the MACRO pins. The forwarded clock now leaves through the same kept `mux2_2` as every data lane (`pt_out_mux`); in AC1_R4 it went through a synthesized oai21_1 and arrived **~3.6 ns after the data at ss** (more than one 3.2 ns UI; FPGA training could not have centred it). **Demand on top-level/pads:** route D2H data and forwarded clock from the macro pins to their pads length-matched (budget the residual skew inside the FPGA's ±UI/2 training window; 0.64 ns is spent inside the macro). (2) `ct_det_clk` is a divided CLOCK to the wired detector, constrained as a generated clock, not as data with an output delay; the consumer must treat it as a clock (no sampling of it against `ct_det_clk_in`). (3) Wired PRBS/pattern words now leave through the core's wire lane registers (`wire_low` falling / `wire_high` rising), like payload: the word serialized at a WORD_CLK40 rising edge is the word consumed by `wire_tx_request` at that edge (serializer-correct pairing; fixes the ff-corner hold on `wire_tx_data[19:0]`). No change to the wired TX owner's interface. (4) Flow now optimizes against the characterized in-window Liberty (ss_032C_3v15 setup, ff_022C/ff_032C hold), which contains 115 of the PDK's cells (no addf/addh, dffnq, dffnsnq, icg, clkbuf_12/20); any future hand-instantiated cell must be one of them (re-run libchar.py otherwise). |

## rf_rx baseband (fork B, 2026-11-11): DCOC / RC-17 / GX-1 attempt, NOT published
| ID | Change | Affects | Action requested |
|---|---|---|---|
| RC-17b | **42 dB CM instability in the PUBLISHED bbfilter_i extraction.** The extracted op at PGA 42 dB converges only by pseudo-transient (gmin and source stepping fail). A power-up transient (VDD ramp, input CM 1.75 V) stays latched after 200 µs at tt/ss/ff (out CM 3.28 V/−1.7 V). Forcing atb_vcm with an ideal source removes the latch, so the CM reference (100 kΩ sense, ~15 OTA inputs, 672 fF parasitic) closes a CM loop with the ×160 PGA gain. The published "0.755 V CM shift at gain 16" is this latch, not a gain error. Fix that was tried: a unity-gain OTAF follower drives atb_vcm (sense node moved to `vcms`). It cured the CM latch in extraction (atb_vcm 1.651 V), but see RC-17c. | rf_rx (owner) | Don't rely on the 42 dB code until this is fixed. |
| RC-17c | **Re-layout iterations destabilise the filter (auto-placer).** Three relayouts were each LVS/DRC clean (area 536–562k µm² vs 494.6k published, budget 430k). Each one moved the virtual-ground parasitics by ±50 %. (a) Inlined DAC + GX-1 wide TGs: 0 dB f3dB 13.3 tt / 8.0 ss MHz, fails. (b) PGA-as-macro + CC_LEAD_GC=0 + vcm buffer: 0 dB 21.8 tt / 16 ss / 28 ff MHz, but peaking 2–4 dB at tt/ff with per-process qt, and at 42 dB unstable (peaking up to +37 dB, DC point saturated). The schematic was fine in every case. | rf_rx (owner), coordinator | The baseband filter needs a hand-floorplanned (or constrained-placement) layout with the integrator virtual grounds kept local before further feature adds. |
| GX-1 status | **Wide code with the current OTAs fails peaking.** CU0 split 9→7 µm (C −40 %), extracted 12 corners at 0 dB: f3dB ss 20.2–21.7 / tt 26.4–27.2 / ff 33.8–34.7 MHz. Peaking at the best Q-trim code (qt3) is 0–1.2 dB at ss, 2.6–4.1 dB at tt and 8.6–10 dB at ff, against ≤ 1 dB. The wide TGs also cost reset-mode bandwidth (see (a)). Needs either more Q-trim range in wide mode (a third, stronger damping bit) or more OTA GBW. | rf_rx, digital (1 bit, not yet) | No digital change until rf_rx publishes. |
| DCOC-F | **Fine DCOC design (schematic-proven, not laid out clean).** `pt_bbf_dcdac`: sign + 8-bit magnitude current DAC into the PGA stage-1 summing nodes through a 100 kΩ isolator inside the PGA macro. The isolator is noise-free because it is in series with a current source. Schematic results: LSB 0.405/0.465/0.339 mV at s1 (tt/ss/ff), FS ±106/124/88 mV, monotonic at the 127→128 step (0.55–0.75 mV), ≈ 6 mV LSB at the ADC at 42 dB (0.7 % FS). That meets RF-RX-DCOC once the layout is stable. Source: scratch/rf-bb/netlists.py.proposal. | rf_rx; digital: 9 bits `bbfilter.offset_{i,q}` (sign-magnitude, reset 0) per path (cf. RXR-D3) | Digital may reserve the 9-bit fields. The analog side is still open. |

Every pad-tracking device 6 V; termination 10 × nfet_06v0 20/0.7 + ppolyf_u 16 × 1.48 µm plus a **new 3-bit 6 V trim bank on `TERM_TRIM0..2`** (= trim[k] AND term_en, PD27 convention); FS fast-off 16 µm, FS control 8 µm; on network-gc-d2. Magic DRC 0, LVS + device audit match (706), KLayout main 0 / antenna 0. **HBM 2 kV 5.04–5.41 V, 0 of 593 MOS over the screen**; ±100 mA 2.37–2.62 V; unpowered peer 0.30–0.49 µA. USB (extracted, calibrated codes tt 4 / ss 7 / ff 0): **HS termination 44.1–47.5 Ω all corners** (spec 40.5–49.5; the published 3.3 V cell is 46 / 53 / 40 Ω, i.e. already out at ss and ff because ppolyf_u spreads ±16 %), C 2.64–2.67 pF (≤ 2.7), FS rise 5.9–11.5 / fall 6.0–8.7 ns (4–20), **FS rise/fall matching 1.13–1.18 tt, 1.27–1.32 ss, 0.99–1.04 ff (spec 0.90–1.11: fails tt/ss; published 1.23–1.48 everywhere)**, FS high at ss/3.15 V 3.10 V (published 2.80). Outline +78 µm deep (1590 µm). | digital, assembly, wired RX/USB, user | **Digital:** `usb.term_trim[2:0]` (reset tt code 4) AND term_en, plus a calibration of the code (none exists). **Assembly:** accept +78 µm depth and 3 new boundary ports. **USB owner/user:** accept the FS matching residual (better than today at every corner) or resize (rise tracks the 6 V PMOS corner, fall the poly/NMOS: one size cannot match both at ss). Pads publishes v9 on those three answers. |
| PD33 | **pt_pll_vreg area, coordinator URGENT (v5 over-full by ≈ 2.15 mm²; 2 × 0.24 mm² does not fit). Answer: one dual-output cell ≈ 0.12 mm² for both rings (≈ 0.06 per ring), same regulation as today; needs a new dense layout (not built yet).** Evidence `analog/pll_vreg/evidence/pd28-vreg-area-options.json` (schematic sweeps with the PDK models plus the extracted r9 ring). Findings: (1) **the area is guard-ring overhead, not device area**: the pass device (native, W 6000 / L 2.7) drawn as 60 single-finger PCells is 0.058 mm²; the same device as one nf = 60 PCell measures 199 × 103 µm = **0.021 mm²**. (2) **The pass device cannot shrink**: at L 1.8 (native minimum) PSRR drops 2.5–3.6 dB; W 2000–4000 pushes ss/3.05 V dropout to 0.45–0.55 V (VOUT 2.50–2.60 V, below the ring's ss need); W 6000 / L 2.7 stays. (3) **The gate cap sets PSRR; Cout and the divider do not**: Cg 15,000 → 7,500 µm² costs 3 dB; Cout 10,000 → 3,000 µm² costs ≤ 0.4 dB; divider 3 MΩ → 300 kΩ (54 → 6 poly units) costs 0 dB. (4) **One regulator with a shared output is NOT recommended**: pll_tx and pll_cdr run at the same nominal rate (± ppm), so ring current at f_A on a shared VOUT injects into ring B within its pulling range. **Proposal `pt_pll_vreg2`**: one divider, one OTA and one gate cap (30,000 µm², shared VG); two native followers (nf = 60 PCell each), one inside the OTA loop and one open-loop on the same VG; each with its own Cout 3,000 µm² and BYP PMOS. A ring sees the other only through VG (Cgd / Cg ≈ −30 dB), not through a shared output. **Schematic, open-loop (worst) output:** dropout 0.29–0.48 V; PSRR worst 10–200 MHz **−23.3 / −24.4 / −24.3 dB** (tt/ss/ff; published packed cell extracted −23.1 / −24.0 / −24.0); extracted r9 ring at 78 MHz / 60 mV: **DJ 2.54 / 1.93 / 2.71 ps** (tt / ss / ff at 771 MHz), spur −46.0 / −48.4 / −45.5 dBc → ≤ 4.1 ps at the A2 90 mV case (< 5 ps). Noise: same OTA and divider as today, and the gate pole (Cg 30,000 µm², about 150 pF) filters both; divider current rises from 1 to 10 µA. **Area estimate** (measured PCells + 25 % routing): 2 × 0.022 pass + 0.033 Cg + 2 × 0.005 Cout + 2 × 0.003 BYP + 0.003 OTA/divider ≈ 0.096 → **≈ 0.12 mm² total (−0.36 mm² vs 2 × 0.241)**. Off-chip decap was not needed: Cout is not the area driver, and every spare pin is booked. | coordinator, assembly, digital, pads | **Coordinator:** approve `pt_pll_vreg2`. Pads will then build it: an nf = 60 native PCell with hand-drawn S/D/G straps as a hard sub-macro (the PCell does not strap fingers: checked, nf = 5 extracts as 5 separate devices), plus the convkit rest, then extracted PSRR, ring and start-up reruns. Not started, because it is a few hours of layout. **Fallback if no time:** regulate only pll_tx (one packed 0.241 mm² cell, −0.241) and put pll_cdr on the raw rail (A3 DJ ≈ 29–33 ps pk ≈ 9 % UI at 3.24 Gb/s inside the CDR sampling; wired RX must accept that). **Assembly:** reserve ≈ 0.12 mm² (about 350 × 350 µm) between pll_tx and pll_cdr; ports `VIN VOUT_TX VOUT_CDR VSS BYP_TX BYP_CDR ATB_OUT ATB_G`. **Digital:** unchanged (`tx_bypass`, `rx_bypass`). |

## wired_rx resume 6 / SLICER+CAPTURE (2026-11-11): slicer r6, IX-10 threshold DACs, capture antenna, safe state

| MT01-W3 | **`pt_slicer` r6: fourth fine trim bit**, ports `trim_{d0,e0,d1,e1,d2,e2,d3,e3}_f3` (+8; 80 trim bits, reset 0). f3 is the new ~0.6-1.6 mV LSB pair; fine code = {f3..f0}, monotonic order is NOT binary (weights f0 1.4-2.0, f1 ~3.6, f2 ~7, f3 0.6-1.6 mV, extracted tt), so the per-die calibration searches the 16 measured codes (as for r4c). | wired_rx, digital (regmap: 8 more bits in `cdr.slicer_trim`) | NOT published (r6 ss regression); extracted max fine gap tt 2.0 / ss 1.59 / ff 2.2 mV |
| IX10-W1 | **IX-10 implemented (wired_rx side).** `pt_slicer` r6: each edge sampler (ws_sae) has an aux PMOS pair (W 6 um/side, on the clocked tail node) whose gates come from a per-sampler select cell (ws_aux): `vsel_<e>_[1:0]` for e0..e3 (+8 ports): 00 off (gates pulled to VDD, no current), 01 VTH_A, 1x VTH_B. New analog lines `VTAP VTAN VTBP VTBN` (+4 ports) from the new block **`pt_vthdac`** (ports `VDD_WIRE_SW VSS vth_en A[7:0] B[7:0] VTAP VTAN VTBP VTBN`): two 8-bit offset-binary current-steering DACs (code 128 = +0.5 LSB above zero, i.e. sign + 7-bit magnitude, 255 steps), self-biased, 0 current with vth_en=0. **Interpretation of "signed 7-bit"**: sign + 7-bit magnitude (8 register bits per DAC); a two's-complement 7-bit DAC cannot meet LSB <= 4 mV with range >= +/-250 mV (64 steps x 4 mV = 256 mV leaves no PVT margin). Registers needed (digital, IX-11 binds them): `cdr.vth_a[7:0]`, `cdr.vth_b[7:0]` (reset 128), `cdr.vth_sel_e0..e3[1:0]` (reset 0), `cdr.vth_en` (reset 0). Integration: route VTAP/VTAN/VTBP/VTBN (quasi-static, settle <= 50 ns) from pt_vthdac to pt_slicer, keep them off clock routes. | wired_rx, digital (regs + IX-11 fast path), integration (4 analog nets, 1 new block) | NOT published; tt range ±393–412 mV, LSB 3.5 mV; DAC ext settling 97–109 ns (fails) |
| W6-SAFE | **Capture safe state is a system state**: with `pt_wrx_cap.EN = 0` the digital must also hold `slicer_en = 0` (sampler outputs S0..7 forced low by pt_slicer) and, when the wired RX is off, `ptrim_en = 0` (CKP0..7 low). Measured extracted (r5h): EN=0 with S static and CKP toggling at 625 MHz **9.66 uA** (limit 10 uA, outputs <= 0.022 V); EN=0 with S toggling (slicer left running) 238 uA (unchanged stress case, not a supported state). In-block alternative rejected: an EN gate on S0..7 adds two gate delays to the RCS=1 re-capture path that has no margin at 834 MHz ss. | digital (enable sequencing: slicer_en <= slicer_en_reg & cap_en, ptrim_en likewise), wired_rx | |

## Digital timing closure TC6 (2026-11-11, digital macro owner)

| ID | Change | Owners affected | Open |
|---|---|---|---|
| DT-1 | **RF TX outputs registered at the macro edge; budget against the opposite rf_tx_clk edge (coordinator decision).** `rf_tx_sample[23:0]`, `rf_tx_valid`, `rf_mute` now leave from rising-edge `rf_tx_clk` flops (`pt_rf_out_reg` in `rtl/pt_digital.sv`). Bypass mode: a one-entry valid/ready output register; the port protocol is unchanged (a sample transfers on an `rf_tx_clk` rising edge with `rf_tx_request && rf_tx_valid`, an untaken sample is held, the bus is all-zero when nothing valid is loaded) but the port latency is **+1 `rf_tx_clk`**. Halfband (80 MHz) mode: plain one-clock delay of the interpolator stream. `rf_mute` is a one-clock-delayed copy of the core mute (asserts no later than one clock after the core). Disable clears valid / sets mute asynchronously. SDC: the outputs must be valid **`RFTX_DAC_MARGIN` = 3.0 ns before the falling `rf_tx_clk` edge at the macro's clock pin** (DAC captures on the opposite edge; the macro's own clock insertion counts against it; spec/design-assumptions.md row "RF TX macro-output -> DAC margin"). **In halfband mode (rf_tx_clk 12.5 ns) this leaves 6.25 − 3.0 = 3.25 ns for clock insertion + clock-to-Q (~2 ns at ss) + output buffer, which the GF180 7-track cells cannot meet: opposite-edge capture is not feasible at 80 MHz.** | RF/converters (DAC capture edge, margin number), assembly (rf_tx_clk skew macro pin vs DAC pin) | RF/assembly to confirm the 3.0 ns margin; coordinator decision for the 80 MHz halfband mode (DAC captures on the same edge one period later, or halfband mode signed off at a lower DAC rate) |
| DT-2 | **Wired TX output stage (internal pipeline, coordinator decision; WT20/WT21 re-checked).** `wire_tx_valid` and `wire_idle` are registered, and the 40-bit word passes one register (`lane_q`) before the existing launch flops, as a one-entry valid/ready stage. Port protocol unchanged (a word transfers on a `WORD_CLK40` rise with `wire_tx_request && wire_tx_valid`), data and valid both one `WORD_CLK40` later, and the **WT20 launch is unchanged**: D0..D19 from falling-edge flops (`wire_low`), D20..D39 from rising-edge flops (`wire_high`), both loaded from `lane_q`; valid/idle from rising-edge flops. WT21 (core ≤ 2.5 Gb/s, `WORD_CLK40` ≥ 16 ns) leaves the SDC's 12.3 ns `wtx` target as margin; the SDC is not relaxed. | wired TX (none: same pins, same launch edges) | none |

## wired_rx resume 7 / DETECT (fork DET7, 2026-11-11): pt_detect r7 (HBM secondary protection, USB level shift)

| ID | Change | Affects | Ask |
|---|---|---|---|
| DT7-1 | **`pt_detect` r7, ports unchanged.** (1) TX side (PD29 item 4): local secondary protection per leg: TX_x → poly R (6 kΩ injection branch TXF, 20 kΩ sense branch TXS) → node clamped by a string of 7 p+/nwell junction diodes to VSS (pfet_06v0 with S=D=anode, G=B=cathode, own well) plus an off nfet_06v0 (negative clamp); every device on TXF/TXS is 6 V. No rail diode: a TXF→VDD diode would back-feed an unpowered island from a 3.63 V TX pad (`tb_safe unpowered`). (2) Receiver-detect injection mirror 10→12× and its 6 V switch 20→40 µm (fixes the ss failure: 61→≥85 µA, TX pad > 0.8 V Vse). (3) USB squelch/disconnect level shift: matched 400 µA PMOS source followers (own wells) and a floating reference VTHP/VREFN = CM ± Vthr/2 around the followers' CM (5T-OTA buffer), Vthr = poly ratio × the VDD-ratio ladder tap (OTA V-to-I). Threshold CM sensitivity −0.05…0.5 V: r6 32 %/V → r7 ≈ 2 %/V. **Open: disconnect at CM 0.5 V fails at ss/3.15 V (gating); the threshold is kick-back (wiring-C) sensitive; architecture fix proposed in README Resume 7 / DETECT.** **Timing: DIFF_POS/DIFF_NEG are valid 100 ns after `det_en` rises** (reference/bias settling); SE_P/SE_N and receiver detect unchanged. Supply ≈ +1.0 mA while det_en=1 (followers 0.8 mA). | digital (ignore DIFF_* for 100 ns after det_en; no port change), assembly (new outline, see README), pads (PD29 screen) | **Digital:** squelch/disconnect FSM masks DIFF_* for ≥ 100 ns (≥ 13 WORD_CLK at 125 MHz) after det_en. **Pads:** refresh `pd28-core-gate-hbm-screen.json` for `wire_tx_detect` / `wirepad_rx_core` from `scratch/wired_rx/r7detect/hbm/`. |

(2) **No HS termination calibration path.** No single code passes every corner (ss needs ≥ 6, ff ≤ 2); at the reset code the termination is 52.3–54.5 Ω at ss and 37.1–38.3 Ω at ff. | W7-GEAR | **`pt_wrx_cap` r7: the 20→40 gearbox (wc_gear, AC-1 / spec S3) moves into the digital macro (coordinator decision 1, 2026-11-11). Supersedes AC3-W5's `DX0..39`/`WCLK40` and T2's `wire_rx_data[39:0]` ← DX.** **Ports removed:** `DX0..DX39`, `WCLK40`. **Port added:** `WSTB` (word strobe, 3.3 V CMOS output, 4/8 µm inverter, one rising edge per 20-bit word; it is the capture's own word-load pulse, a private copy of GW). Order: `S0..7 CKP0..7 EN LDE RCS B2 B1 PSEL0..2 SLIPP SLIPM DW0..19 EW0..19 SLP SLM UP0..3 DN0..3 WSTB VDD VSS`. **Word:** `DW[19:0]` = 20 consecutive line bits, **DW0 earliest** in line order; `EW[i]` = edge sample between data bits i and i+1 (EW19 straddles into the next word); `SLP/SLM` = full-rate slip flags presented with that word. **Word period = 20 UI** (quarter rate 5 ring cycles, half 10, full 20): ring 480 → 10.42 ns, 625 → 8.00, 742.5 → 6.73, 750 → 6.67, 834 → 6.00 ns (quarter). **Timing at the block pins (extracted, 20 fF on DW/EW, 50 fF on WSTB):** DW/EW change **0.95–1.07 ns after the WSTB rise (tt/27/3.3, q480 and q625), 0.75–0.85 ns at ff/22/3.35 with extracted C × 0.9 (optimistic)**; bus skew (last − first bit) ≤ 0.12 ns; WSTB high time ≈ T/2 + 0.2 ns (1.20 ns at 480, 0.97 at 625, 0.64 at 834) — DW changes at about the WSTB fall, so **the falling edge must not be used**. **Required macro capture:** register `DW/EW/SLP/SLM` on the **WSTB rising edge** (this captures the word loaded at the previous strobe; stream continuity of consecutive strobe samples checked in every bench). Hold budget at the macro flop: (WSTB clock-tree insertion − DW route delay + flop hold) < **0.75 ns** (ff/optimistic pin value; add route skew); setup budget: word period − 1.1 ns (tt) − ss excess (see ss row in README) − route. WSTB is a block-generated clock: constrain as a generated clock of period 20 UI (≥ 6.0 ns at 834, ≥ 8.0 ns at the WT21 625 MHz cap), then CDC (async FIFO) into the macro core as before. **Gear in the macro:** a 1-bit toggle T in the WSTB domain, reset (T=0) while `cap_en`=0 and re-zeroed by each `LDE` word restart; `wire_rx_data[39:0]` = {later word, earlier word}, earlier word in [19:0] (AC3-W5 convention unchanged), valid every second WSTB; spec S3 (40-bit at line/40) now lives entirely inside the macro. **Metastability (slicer r7 request, E samplers metastable by design):** EW passes ≥ 4 ring-rate flop stages in the capture (F'→H/G→Q→O→lane→pair R/W); in addition **the macro must put a second flop on EW[19:0] after the WSTB-rise register** (both in the WSTB domain). Measured TSPC capture-flop τ (schematic ss/32/3.15, keeper flop, 10 fF): **0.19–0.22 ns**. Conservative MTBF at 834 MHz quarter with 1e6 unresolved E events/s, counting only H→Q and Q→O (2 × 0.9 ns) plus the two macro stages (4.6 + 5.5 ns): exp(11.9/0.22)/1e6 ≈ 3e17 s; without the second macro flop ≈ 4e6 s (46 days, insufficient). EW also feeds the PD (UP/DN analog votes): a late vote is one wrong vote, no logic state. **Reset/alignment:** EN=0 holds DW/EW/SLP/SLM/UP/DN/WSTB low (extracted ≤ 0.03 V); after EN rises the first two strobes after `LDE` carry stale words (bench discards them); the word boundary is set by LDE as before. **Block:** 1181.3 × 241.5 µm = **0.285 mm²** (r6 published 0.938 mm²), Magic DRC 0, LVS unique, KLayout main 0 / antenna 0 — **staged, see status**. | digital (macro: WSTB-domain 20-bit register + second EW flop + 2:1 gear + CDC, STA generated clock; regmap unchanged), integration (`chip_assembly` port list: −41 pins +1, re-place 1181 × 242 µm), spec (S3 now macro-internal) | **Status: NOT published** — extracted q480/q625 tt pass, safe 7.3 µA pass, ff-optimistic hold cases pass; **q834 fails** (pre-existing: r5h published also fails ext q834_r1, 3 data / 8 edge errors and wrong PD votes; r7 21/30, ss/834 no words). Needs a coordinator decision on q834 given WT21 (ring ≤ 625 MHz) and SLI7-R (slicer passes only 625 at pessimistic). |

| DT-10 | **POR override strap `por_bypass` (digital, 2026-11-13; integration-plan G3, "no dead chip"). Macro side done; chip level OPEN (needs a pad: user decision).** New macro input **`por_bypass`** (south edge, next to `reset_n_pad`/`por_n` in `verification/digital_hardening/pin_order.cfg`). Macro reset is now `rst_n = reset_n_pad & (por_n \| por_bypass)`: a static level through one OR, no flop / clock / SPI / analog needed. 0 (default) = today's behaviour; 1 = the Edwards POR is out of the reset path and RESET_N alone resets the die (for a POR that never releases). RESET_N always dominates; the strap alone never releases reset. Readback: **RESET_STATUS (0x0D) b2 = live strap level** (read-only, new); `por_flag` (b0) still follows the raw `por_n`, so b0 stuck at 1 with b2 = 1 means the POR is still asserted; `analog_status[4]` unchanged. **Until a pad is bound, `integration/pt_chip.sv` ties `por_bypass = 0`: G3 is NOT fixed at chip level.** `make transceiver-por-bypass-chip` fails while that constant is there (closure tripwire). **Demand on pads/assembly: one INPUT-ONLY strap pad.** All 56 signal sites of the 74-pad ring are assigned (`spec/contract.json` bond_map_current), so a site has to be given up or converted; candidates (user decision): (1) convert the DBG_OBS1 site (`bidir[39]`, north; today `gf180mcu_fd_io__bi_24t`, output only) to `gf180mcu_fd_io__in_c` with PU = 0, PD = 1, the same proven binding as RESET_N, and retire `debug.dbg_obs.sel1` (DBG_OBS0 carries every code; costs one observation pin); (2) the same on WMON (`bidir[24]`) or the TLP/ESD test site (`bidir[25]`). **Rejected after independent review: sharing DBG_OBS1 as a bidirectional pad** (pad Y as strap while the debug mux can still drive it). Reproduced in simulation: with a dead POR and the jumper fitted, any DBG_OBS1 code that drives 0 resets the die through a runt asynchronous pulse; with a healthy POR, a code driving 1 silently removes POR protection during a brownout; code 36 (`rst_n`) closes a combinational loop through the pad. (3) An in-ring bond-option pad decided at assembly (fixed per die, not changeable on the bench) is the weakest option. **Unbonded-safe default (must be shown by pads, not yet shown by anything):** the provider cell's pull-down (PD hard-tied to VDD_CORE, PU to VSS, like `rst_n_pad`) must hold the pad below VIL against pad leakage at ff / 85 °C / 3.63 V: quote pull-down current vs leakage, add the strap pad to the `kl_assemble` / `kl_checks` tie check (today only `rst_n_pad`), and include a floating-strap case in the M6 power-up transient. The provider Verilog model of `in_c` ignores PU/PD, so this cannot be shown in RTL simulation. The strap must NOT come from a register, an island-powered net or anything behind the POR. **Assembly/integration:** replace the constant in `pt_chip.sv`, bind `digital.por_bypass` in the chip netlist; `verification/chip_assembly/netlist.py` has no reset net to any analog block today (if one is ever added it must use `RESET_N & (por_n \| por_bypass)`). Add the chip-level test (`sim/tb_chip_io.sv`: POR forced low + strap pad high ⇒ ID 0x5356; strap low ⇒ no answer). **Board:** DNP jumper / 1 kΩ to VDD on the strap pin; with the strap high a brownout no longer resets the die by itself, so the RESET_N supervisor (B1) is then mandatory and RESET_N must be low during the supply ramp; new entry needed in `spec/board-requirements.md`. **Pad-free alternatives (not built; user may ask for one as a second path if no pad is released):** a RESET_N double-pulse / keyed-sequence detector needs state that nothing initialises when the POR is dead (random power-up state, counter or shift register in the reset path): rejected. A conventional boot strap (one latch, transparent while RESET_N is low, capturing e.g. MOSI = SCLK = CS_N = 1 at RESET_N release) is initialised by RESET_N itself, but puts a latch in the reset path, is re-sampled by RESET_N noise, and the SPI inputs have no pulls (a floating host could set it; that only removes POR protection): more failure modes than the input-only pad, fewer than losing the die. Tests: `verification/check_por_bypass.py` (`sim/tb_por_bypass.sv`: POR stuck low + strap ⇒ ID 0x5356, write/readback, host/core reset domains released; strap low ⇒ held in reset; strap never overrides RESET_N; mutants must fail), regfile formal re-proven with `por_n` free of `rst_n`. Not yet done: post-route gate-level run (macro must be re-hardened with the new pin; the outline LEF has no `por_bypass` pin yet). | **user** (which pad to give up, second path yes/no), pads (strap cell + pull-down proof + tie check), assembly (bind, chip test, LEF pin), board (jumper, B1 supervisor) | none (one OR gate, one status bit) |

Nothing needs the oscillator before SPI is up, the analog safe-state evidence assumes EN = 0, and an oscillator running during the POR hold-off is an avoidable aggressor; the field stays live RW so software can pulse EN low to leave the stuck state (F3). Map hash will change. **Assembly must bind `aosc.EN` to `debug_cfg[246]`** (floating CMOS input today) whichever default is chosen. (F5) Bandgap enable overshoot: proposal a register-file rule (commit rejected when `bgap_ena` goes 0→1 in the same commit as `ref_sel_vbg` = 1 or any `dist_en` = 1), so the 10 µs order is two SPI commits (≥ 64 SCLK periods ≥ 12.8 µs at ≤ 5 MHz); needs the exact field names from the bias owner. (F11) ATB code 4 `monitor_por_threshold`: keep the code number (removing it renumbers every node) but **assembly must leave it unbound or bind it to a buffered logic-level node, never to the POR `vcap`**; the register spec text will say 'reserved'. **SPI top-level routes (G6, `verification/digital_hardening/spi_signoff.py`):** the provider pad Y outputs are weak into the ≈ 2.6 mm routes to the macro (bi_24t: 3.0–6.6 ns delay, up to 4.7 ns edge into 1.05 pF at ss_125C_2v97). Request to pads/assembly: a `clkbuf_8`-class repeater within ≈ 200 µm of the SCLK, CS_N and MOSI pads (edge < 1 ns at the macro), wire C ≤ 1.0 pF and R ≤ 1.4 kΩ per route (the bounds the sign-off assumes), and pulls on the SPI inputs are worth considering (PU = PD = 0 today: a floating host leaves SCLK/CS_N/MOSI floating; RESET_N low still holds the die safe). The macro now assumes 1.05 pF on `spi_miso` / `spi_miso_oe` (SDC `set_load`). **After independent review of the sign-off:** the pad tables were used outside their Liberty range (bi_24t Y `max_capacitance` 0.5 pF, bi_t A `max_transition` 1.0 ns; SCLK macro pin is 0.16–0.24 pF, not 0.05), so the repeater at each SPI pad is a REQUIREMENT, not an option (Y load < 0.1 pF, pad A edge < 1 ns); 2.6 mm is a floor (vertical distance only). Pads: set **CS = 1 (Schmitt) on SCLK and CS_N, PU = 1 on CS_N, PD = 1 on SCLK and MOSI** (today CS = PU = PD = 0: a hi-Z host floats them, and a 4.7 ns edge without hysteresis is a double-clock risk). **Host/board rule (new):** CS_N must stay low for at least half an SCLK period (≥ 100 ns) after the last SCLK falling edge of a transaction: `wr_en` is gated combinationally by CS_N and the register file commits on that falling edge ~6–10 ns after the pad (path not timed; a structural fix in `pt_spi` is queued). SCLK limits are half-cycle: **SCLK high ≥ 71 ns, low ≥ 49 ns** at the slow bound with OCV (so ≤ 5 MHz needs 36–64 % duty); these numbers come from a post-CTS database with estimated parasitics and must be redone on the routed macro. SPI-domain hold does NOT yet survive ±8 % on-chip variation (−0.07 ns at the foundry ff library; all failing paths are synchronizer stage pairs, the SPI core flops have ≥ +0.45 ns): hold is frequency-independent, so no SCLK rate is signed off until the re-hardened macro meets the target (≥ 0 at ±14 %, ≥ +0.15 ns at ±8 %). Bring-up ≤ 1 MHz. | timing/bias (F2, F5 field names), assembly (aosc.EN, ATB code 4, SPI repeaters), pads (SPI pulls), board (SCLK ≤ 5 MHz) | SCLK ≤ 5 MHz (was 10 MHz bench rate) |

Only number available today (scratch/digital-timing-close/pwr/run14_tt.log; TC14 post-CTS, placement-estimated RC, tt 3.3 V, vectorless): **clock networks alone 188 mW = 57 mA with EVERY clock running at its constraint frequency at once** (0.73 nC per 12.8 ns), which no mode does (wired and RF trees are exclusive, counters and SCLK idle), so it is a loose upper bound on the clock part, not a mode number; the vectorless data-path figure (0.81 W at activity 0.25) is not usable. Cell area today 1.91 mm² post-CTS (68 %); whitespace ≈ 0.90 mm² → ≈ 0.49 nF at 0.541 fF/µm² if all of it were decap cells; at 2.45 mm² it would be ≈ 0.29 nF. (4) 39.0625 MHz ADC/DAC clock (= core clock / 2): the transport is rate-agnostic below its 40 MS/s limit (ingress FIFO cannot overrun), the 2× interpolator and packers do not change; cost on the digital side is only the sample-rate constant in the host/FPGA software and, if the macro has to make the clock, one divider flop on hcore with a forwarded output like `rf_tx_clk_out`. A synchronous sample clock also makes the RF ingress/egress FIFO occupancy static.
