### WT24 status (pads, 2026-11-12, in progress — NOT closed)
- Bench `scratch/pads-wt24/bench.py` (real extracted stress-safe TX pad as load, MAIN_P gates 8x20/0.28 um; +25 % device C, +15 % extracted C, Rg 200 ohm/10 um; 750 um output route pi): ideal driver PASS (120 ps -> 112-117 ps), old buffer FAIL (tt: 400 ps -> 230 ps, reproduces WT24; ss pess: everything <800 ps lost, tpd 970 ps).
- Instantiated version: the OLD 0.115 mm2 `generated/steering-buffer/` (attached-core-area, wtx_fallback, wired_tx sim_integration). The compact cell is referenced nowhere. sim_integration also loads the outdated `wired-transmit-extracted-c.spice` pad (347 dev) instead of `stress-safe-families/wired-transmit/physical.spice` (576 dev).
- Schematic candidate 4-stage n=4/10/24/56 um, P/N=2 (input 12 um, sized for the CMD NAND ~1 kohm at ss): ss/32/3.15 pess passes 400/800 ps, 300 ps at -57 ps; tt passes >=200 ps. Delay ~0.5 ns ss pess, P/N skew <= 2 ps.
- <=120 ps at ss pessimistic is NOT reached by any CMOS chain tried (2- and 4-stage, FO 2.3-6): ~120 ps/stage at ss pess into the 160 um switch gates. Closing it needs a smaller pad switch gate C or a low-swing/CML pre-driver; owner decision required. Layout/extraction/integration bench/review not yet done.

### WT24 r2: CML pre-driver + right-sized steering switches (pads/WT24 owner, 2026-11-12, IN PROGRESS: schematic bench only)
Bench `scratch/pads-wt24/bench2.py` (load = published extracted TX pad, switch devices scaled by S; +25 % device C, +15 % extracted C, Rg 200 ohm/10 um; ss/32/3.15). Checks: ideal full-swing gate driver PASS; a 0.3 V-swing broken driver gives 0.076 V vs 0.454 V, so it fails on amplitude (absolute floor 0.40 V pk = 0.8 Vppd added; it was declared before the CML results).
- **Finding (affects the "size for saturation" premise):** the stack PAD (CM ~1.15 V with on-die term) / 6 V cascode / switch / 20/1 tail has no headroom. The switches run in TRIODE, not saturation. The off-side gate must go below ~0.8 V and the on-side above ~2.6 V, so CML swing must be ~2 V, not ~0.5 V. Effective gate load is ~0.8-0.9 pF per leg at S=1 (Miller incl.) and ~0.45 pF at S=0.5.
- **Candidate:** one CML pre-driver per branch (MAIN, POST), with an nfet pair driven by CMD_P/CMD_N (2 x 20 um) and I_tail 9.5 mA. Loads: RL 220 ohm per leg plus a shared Rcm 55 ohm (high 2.6 V, low 0.55 V). Switches: S=0.5 (8 x 10/0.28 um per leg).
- **ss pess:** 309 ps bits pass (295-300 ps out) at R +/-20 %, and ff/22/3.35 low-C passes too. 250 ps passes; 150 ps fails at ss (passes at ff).
- **Amplitude:** 0.36-0.38 V pk at ss pess vs the 0.40 V floor (full-CMOS gates at S=0.5: 0.418). Not closed; options are a +5-10 % DAC code or S=0.6 with I_tail ~11 mA.
- Cost: ~19 mA for MAIN + POST at 3.3 V (TMDS branch unused per X5, gates tied low). Area estimate is small (~2 x 20 um pair + poly R + tail), not drawn yet.
- **Ask, pads (EM agent, also edits steering_buffer.py):** resize MAIN/POST switch devices to S=0.5 (10 um fingers) in `wired-transmit`. The primary ESD and 1 kV path is unchanged (core-side devices only). Re-extract and re-run LVS/HBM screen. Gate C to publish: ~0.45 pF/leg effective in operation.
- **Ask, wired TX:**
  - (1) CMD_P/CMD_N must be complementary; the CML switches at their crossing, so CMD duty cycle and skew go straight to the pad.
  - (2) Provide EN_MAIN/EN_POST (or ISLAND_OFF) to the pre-driver so its tail turns off and the outputs pull to 0 when disabled. With CML, both CMD low no longer means "switches off".
  - (3) `analog/wired_tx/sim_integration.py` uses the STALE pad netlist `wired-transmit-extracted-c.spice` (347 devices). The current one is `stress-safe-families/wired-transmit/physical.spice` (576 devices, extra port ATB_ESD_RAIL).
- **Ask, assembly:** the inventory (`pads/generated/attached-core-area.json`, `integration/wtx_fallback.py`) still instantiates the OLD 0.115 mm2 `steering-buffer/` x6 (0.69 mm2). Swap in `steering-buffer-compact/` (0.0096 mm2, electrically equivalent per equivalence.json) now as the interim. The CML pre-driver replaces both once WT24 r2 is published.
- **Still to do:** transistor tail + replica bias tracking the poly R, enable/off-state gating, poly R models, layout, extraction, package bench (sim_integration), independent review.

### WT24 r3 (pads/WT24 owner, 2026-11-12): 0.6x switch + ~11 mA CML. Status: ideal-tail PASS, transistor-tail NOT closed
- Ideal R-tracking tail (I proportional to 1/R), S=0.6, RL 195 / Rcm 35, about 11 mA: ss pess 309 ps PASS at R nominal / -20 % / +20 % (293-299 ps out). Settled amplitude 0.410 / 0.402 / 0.414 V pk against the 0.40 floor. Without R tracking (fixed 11 mA), R +/-20 % gives 0.383-0.398 and FAILS. NOTE: the 0.40 V floor was declared in r2 but only coded into bench2's pass flag here; the r2 PASS labels were timing-only.
- Transistor version (`scratch/pads-wt24/cmlx.py`): replica-mirror tail, ppolyf_u loads (res_ss/res_ff two-sided), pfet header (300 um) gated by EN, ENB pull-downs on both outputs, mirror off when EN=0.
  - The tail and pair have no headroom. The output low level must be about 0.6 V, so the pair source sits at 0.2-0.3 V and the tail runs in triode. Current no longer tracks 1/R.
  - tt: PASS (0.442 V, 309 -> 298-302 ps). ff/22/3.35 low-C: PASS.
  - ss pess, 1 kohm CMD source: FAIL (309 -> 275-281 ps).
  - ss pess, 300 ohm CMD source: timing PASS at both res corners. Amplitude 0.414 V (res_ss) PASS; 0.358 V (res_ff, low level 0.90 V) FAIL.
- A resistor-loaded pair with no tail is worse: nfet_03v3 Idsat is only about 300 uA/um at ss, so the pull-down saturates. Any stage that sinks about 11 mA with a low level near 0.6 V needs 40-100 um of nfet, too much input C for the CMD NAND (about 1 kohm at ss).
- **Wired TX:** CMD source impedance ~1 kohm at ss limits the pre-driver input (bench `rsrc`). Need <= ~300 ohm, either a 3x CMD output stage or a local CMOS pre-stage inside the pre-driver (pads will add one if wired TX prefers).
- **Power budget:** +2 x ~11 mA (MAIN + POST pre-drivers, 9-13 mA each across corners) replaces the old 6-buffer dynamic current (~2 mA). Net about +20 mA at 3.3 V, NOT +3.5 mA. Coordinator: the "+3.5 mA" estimate is wrong; budget about +20 mA.
- **Next:** local CMOS pre-stage; larger low-side / R-tracking fix for res_ff (an op-amp replica setting the low level, or RL 225); then layout, extraction, package bench, review.

### WT24 r4 (pads/WT24 owner, 2026-11-12): CML pre-driver laid out; tt package bench PASS; ss-pessimistic FAILS, input-drive limited
- **Topology change vs r3 (deviates from the "tail + replica loop" instruction; reason below).**
  - Replica-loop version (`scratch/pads-wt24/cmlr.py`): the loop holds the low level at 0.50-0.52 V across tt/ss/ff x res_ss/res_ff when the pair is >= 64-80 um. That pair loads the input inverters enough that ss 309 ps fails (257-276 ps out).
  - Adopted instead, `wt24_q6`: resistor-loaded nfet pair with NO tail. The pair is 2 x 36 um, sourced to VSS and driven by local input inverters 7n/21p, so the wired TX interface is unchanged (CMD_P/CMD_N in).
  - Other parts: RL 190 ohm and Rcm 12 ohm (ppolyf_u); 300 um pfet header on EN; ENB pull-downs.
  - The low level is set by the pair Ron/RL ratio (0.5-0.9 V across corners) and the high level is 2.8-3.0 V. Current tracks 1/R.
- **Schematic results, S=0.6, pessimistic C/Rg:**
  - ss/32/3.15, 1 kohm CMD stand-in: 309 ps -> 291/297 ps (PASS) at res_ss and 296/299 ps at res_ff.
  - ff/22/3.35 low-C: PASS at both resistor corners.
  - Off state (EN=0): gates held at -0.05..+0.03 V, pad 0 V.
  - Amplitude at res_ff is 0.363 V. The ideal full-swing driver at that corner gives 0.388 V (94 %), so the shortfall is the pad's own poly termination at low R, which the termination trim covers. The absolute 0.40 V floor is NOT met at res_ff; reported, not relaxed.
- **Layout** (`scratch/pads-wt24/lay_q6`, array generator, rebuild image b60278092f08 because the locked sha 9253a51f is absent on this host): Magic DRC 0, device graph matches, GDS roundtrip clean. 14,124 um2 per pre-driver; 2 needed = 0.028 mm2, vs 0.69 mm2 for today's 6 x 0.115 mm2 instances. KLayout DRC, LVS report and EM review not yet run.
- **Extracted pre-driver, isolated bench, ss pess:** FAIL (309 -> 223-237 ps). Extracted INP/INN wiring is ~40 fF and GN/GP ~40 fF. With a 100 ohm source it passes (285/292 ps); at 300 ohm it gives 282/289 ps (FAIL). The 1 kohm CMD drive into the cell is the limiter.
- **Package bench** (sim_integration with the corrected pad netlist, MAIN+POST pre-drivers, S=0.6 pad, P4, 2.5 Gb/s, code 200):

  | Pre-driver | Corner | Errors | Eye height | Eye width | Crossing spread |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | schematic | tt | 0 | 613 mV | 335 ps | 65 ps |
  | schematic | ss/32/3.15 | 0 | 476 mV | 294 ps | — |
  | extracted | tt | 0 | 595 mV | 327 ps | 73 ps |
  | extracted + pessimistic C/Rg | ss/32/3.15 | **3** | 35 mV | — | — |

  The old buffer path had 320-380 ps crossing spread with 6-40 errors at tt.
- **Ask, wired TX (internal sizing only, no interface change):** the CMD_x output NAND, about 1 kohm at ss, can't drive the 2.5 Gb/s path at ss pessimistic. Make it ~3x stronger (<= ~300 ohm), or confirm pads should add a deeper in-cell input taper.
- **Power** (spec/budgets/power_budget.py, wired ceiling 292 mA):
  - Pre-driver current per branch: 9.9-14.9 mA across corners and resistor corners.
  - Wired cases today: allocation margin +47 mA, with-digital-requirement margin +16.9 mA, evidence bound already -4.9 mA.
  - MAIN+POST adds ~+22 mA net (vs ~2 mA for the old buffers): with-digital margin goes to about -5 mA (BREAKS) and the evidence bound to about -27 mA.
  - Trade: disable the POST pre-driver (EN_POST=0) whenever POST code = 0 (no de-emphasis; TMDS; USB). That leaves +~11 mA, with-digital margin about +6 mA.
  - De-emphasis (PCIe -3.5/-6 dB) and MLT-3 (POST_POL) need both pre-drivers and still break the with-digital case by ~5 mA. Coordinator decision.
- **Remaining:** stronger CMD drive or an in-cell input taper, then re-extract; pads switch resize to S=0.6 (EM agent); KLayout DRC/LVS/EM at 85 C; independent review. NOT done.

PD8 forms 8 D/E XOR vote pairs per ring cycle from capture A (native phases) and capture B (blended phases), weights the proportional kick by 8/ntrans (3-bit LUT on the transition count, so the P range no longer depends on transition density) and drives the 4 FU/FD legs; the same votes go unweighted to pt_cdr UP/DN (integral). ### WT24 r5 (pads/WT24 owner, 2026-11-12): package bench 0 errors at ss pessimistic; swing at low-R and power NOT closed -> hand-off
- **Deeper in-cell input taper:** tried and rejected. 2- and 3-stage CMOS tapers inside the cell (2/5/13, 3/9, 2/6/16 um nfet, with 40 fF wiring per node) swallow 309 ps pulses at ss pessimistic: nothing out, or only 68 ps at the trailing high pulse. The single inverter stays best: 6n/21p into a 2 x 36 um pair, RL 175 ohm, Rcm 12 ohm (`scratch/pads-wt24/cands/s4n.spice`).
- **Layout coupling fix:** the extracted loss came from parallel M5 trunk coupling: INP-INN 11.4 fF and GP-INN 12.8 fF, both Miller-doubled. I added an optional `signal_gap_um` to `analog/pads/termination_buffer_array_layout.generate`; the default 1.0 keeps every existing cell bit-identical. With a 5 um gap, INP-INN drops to 2.4 fF and GP-INN to 8.3 fF.
- **Layout `lay_s4g`:** Magic DRC 0, device graph match, GDS roundtrip clean, 16,591 um2 per pre-driver (2 = 0.033 mm2).
- **Extracted + pessimistic C/Rg, ss/32/3.15, P4, 2.5 Gb/s:**
  - Package bench (sim_integration with the real pt_wiredtx pex driving CMD): **0 errors / 221 bits**, eye 243 mV, width 232 ps, crossing spread 168 ps. The pad is the published netlist with switches scaled to 0.6x, not yet re-laid out and NOT pessimized.
  - tt package bench, extracted: 0 errors, eye 595 mV, 327 ps.
  - Isolated pulse-width check at 1 kohm, ss pess: 309 ps -> 250-262 ps. This still FAILS the +/-25 ps pulse-fidelity limit.
- **Low-R swing:** the TX pad has NO termination trim. No TERM_TRIM on `wired-transmit` (only the USB pad and hspad have one), so "the trim restores it" does not hold.
  - At res_ff: ideal full-swing gates give 0.388 V (code ~200) and 0.41 V at higher DAC current.
  - The CML cell gives 0.359-0.374 V, unchanged with more DAC current. Its low level rises to 0.95 V at low R (RL falls, pair Ron does not), so the off-side switch leaks.
  - **FAIL.** Options: (a) a TX termination trim in the pad; (b) a wider pair, which costs input load and therefore timing; (c) a 1-2 bit RL trim in the cell, which would also give the fast-corner current trim below.
- **Power, NOT closed:**
  - POST gating at code 0 is approved; that needs digital to drive EN_POST = (post code != 0) AND EN, a new digital->pad control.
  - De-emphasis/MLT-3 still needs ~5 mA. Pre-driver current is 9.9 mA (ss, res_ss) up to 14.9 mA (ff, res_ff); an RL/current trim at fast corners would save ~3-5 mA but is not designed.
  - Unused-block power-down in those modes is not audited.
- **power-budget.json:** my `power_budget.py --check` run regenerated it from another owner's uncommitted power_budget.py and input edits (RF direction recipe). I restored the HEAD version. The regenerated copy is at `scratch/pads-wt24/power-budget.regenerated.json`. The RF/power owner must regenerate it after committing their .py.
- **Hand-off to pads (EM agent):**
  1. Resize the MAIN/POST/TMDS switch devices to 0.6x (12 um fingers) in `wired-transmit`, then re-extract, re-run LVS and the HBM screen.
  2. Publish the pre-driver cell `lay_s4g` (KLayout DRC, LVS report, EM at 85 C: TOP/TOP2/OUTP/OUTN trunks carry 10-15 mA).
  3. Decide the low-R swing fix (a/b/c).
  4. Independent review.
  The assembly row from r2 still applies: swap the 6 old steering buffers out of attached-core-area.

Ports/netlist unchanged (VDD VSS REF BIAS OUT ENABLE); readiness unchanged. **Outline grows: 167.2 × 370.3 → 156.8 × 479.6 µm (0.0752 mm²).** Compensation MIM now in the repo (`pads/generated/termination-buffer-compensation-mim/`). | assembly | **Assembly:** re-place the termination buffer: reservation 170 × 375 → ≥ 160 × 483 µm; re-check that the block still fits. |
| RING-ASYM | **`ct_ring9` asymmetric reset (r9w, wired RX resume 7, 2026-11-12; independent review of r9v found a common-mode latch).** With every P/N clamped low at EN=0 the pseudo-differential ring starts symmetric and, at the full pessimistic set (+25 % device C, `pess.py`), settles in the common-mode latch (P_k == N_k; reviewer's ss/32/2.69 HI 2.05 V check: no oscillation; r9d had the same issue). Fix: the stage-0 N1 clamp gate goes to P1 instead of ENB (`fastcells.ring9`, `ASYM_RESET`): off while P1 is clamped, so N1 is driven high -> differential start; in operation a 0.5 um extra keeper. Same cell bbox/pins (83.7 x 49.1 um), 72 devices. **Extracted (r9w top re-extracted):** full pessimistic ss/32/2.69 HI 2.05 V starts, 641 MHz; full pessimistic tt/27/2.925 VCTRL 1.21 541 MHz; nominal ss/32 HI 1.9 V 844.6 MHz (r9v 848.1), phase errors -15.2..+0.1 deg (span 15.3 vs 13.7, ptrim 29). Top: Magic DRC 0, LVS unique, KLayout 0/0; published `analog/timing/generated/pll_ring.*` (r9v backup `scratch/wired_rx/r8impl/r9v_backup/`) and `generated/fast/ct_ring9.*`. **`pt_pll_ring_core` (pll_tx) also instantiates ct_ring9:** its GDS stays valid (same cell outline/pins) but its `pll_ring_core.ext.spice` / LVS / evidence must be regenerated (open, wired RX). Open: closed-loop cold start from VCTRL=0, slow EN/supply ramp, mismatch MC. | timing (pll_ring_core re-extract), assembly (no geometry change) | none |

### WT24 r6 (pads/WT24 owner, 2026-11-12): 2-bit RL trim, 3.24 Gb/s package eye, EN_POST/TRIM to digital
- **Cell `wt24_tr7`** (`scratch/pads-wt24/cands/tr7.spice`, layout `lay_tr7`): Magic DRC 0, device graph match, GDS roundtrip clean, 27,551 um2 per cell (2 = 0.055 mm2).
  - Single input inverter 6n/21p into a 2 x 36 um nfet pair; RL 260 ohm; Rcm 7 ohm; 160 um EN header.
  - 2-bit parallel RL trim per leg: pfet switch + ppolyf_u, bit0 1100 ohm, bit1 550 ohm. Code 3 = lowest RL, used at high R; code 0 = highest RL, used at low R and at fast corners.
- **Bench `bench3.py`, the deciding test:** idealised CMD source (PRBS7, 3.24 Gb/s, 1 kohm stand-in for the CMD NAND, because the current quarter-rate pt_wiredtx core can't make 3.24: its own CMD crossing spread is 291 ps at ss), cell, pad scaled 0.6x and pessimised two-sided, sim_integration's P4 package chain, eye at the far end.
  - Ideal driver: 0 errors, H 0.75 V, W 306 ps. Broken case (cell with a 6 kohm source): 45 errors, so the bench can fail.
  - The pad output is inverted relative to CMD_P (eye scored with either polarity).

  | Corner (two-sided) | Res / trim | Errors | Eye H | Eye W (UI 309 ps) | Pre-driver I |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | ss/32/3.15, high C, Rg | res_ss / c3 | 0 | 0.168 V | 130 ps | 10.5 mA |
  | ss/32/3.15, high C, Rg | res_ff / c0 | 0 | 0.129 V | 131 ps | 11.2 mA |
  | ff/22/3.35, low C | res_ss / c3 | 0 | 0.845 V | 289 ps | 12.9 mA |
  | ff/22/3.35, low C | res_ss / c0 (power trim) | 0 | 0.690 V | 276 ps | 10.9 mA |
  | ff/22/3.35, low C | res_ff / c0 | 0 | 0.640 V | 283 ps | 13.5 mA |

  - At ss the margin is THIN (0.42 UI). The un-trimmed layout `lay_s4g` gets W 252 ps / H 0.47 V at ss pess, so the trim legs plus the taller generated cell cost about 120 ps of eye: OUT 162 vs 98 fF, gate nodes 53 vs 37 fF, TOP 165 vs 60 fF. A hand-compacted layout should recover most of it.
  - The isolated pulse-width check (advisory) still fails at ss pess.
- **Low-R swing:** NOT met. At res_ff with code 0 and the MAIN DAC bias raised (mbias 1.9 -> 3.0): 0.361 -> 0.387 V pk against the 0.40 floor. The ideal full-swing gate driver gets only 0.388-0.41 V there. The trim fixes the gate LOW level (0.64 V) but not the HIGH level (2.73 V, switch Ron). Root cause: the TX pad's untrimmed poly termination at low R.
  - **Pads ask:** add a TX termination trim (as on the USB/hs pads), or accept 0.39 V at the -20 % R corner.
- **Digital ask (new controls):**
  - `EN_POST = EN_MAIN_active AND (post.code != 0)`, and `EN_MAIN` to each pre-driver EN input (or ISLAND_OFF-derived).
  - `wtx.predrv_trim[1:0]`: 2-bit register, shared by MAIN and POST (same die R), default 1, calibrated per die from the pad swing (ATB) or the poly-R monitor. Fast-corner power policy: lowest-current code that still meets swing.
- **Power** (wired ceiling 292 mA; with-digital margin today +16.9 mA, which includes ~2 mA for the old buffers):
  - Pre-driver is 10.5-13.5 mA per branch.
  - MAIN only (POST gated, code 0): about +9-11.5 mA, PASS with ~5-7 mA left.
  - De-emphasis / MLT-3 (both on): about +19-25 mA, FAILS by ~2-8 mA.
  - Savings identified:
    - (a) POST pre-driver always at trim c0 (POST carries code 48-80, so its switches need less overdrive): ~1.5-2 mA, needs a sim.
    - (b) fast-corner c0 on MAIN: ~2 mA at ff.
    - (c) In de-emphasis/MLT-3 the TMDS path is already off (X5), and detect (1.66 mA) can be gated once the link is up.
  - (a)+(b)+(c) is about 5 mA at the fast corner but only about 3.5 mA at ss/res_ff. Owners of detect and of the digital link state must confirm (c). NOT closed.
- **Hand-off to pads (EM agent):**
  - 0.6x switch resize in `wired-transmit`, then re-extract, re-run LVS/HBM, and re-run bench3 with the real resized pad.
  - Publish `lay_tr7` (or a hand-compacted version) with KLayout DRC, a netgen LVS report, and EM at 85 C. OUTP/OUTN trunks are 3 um M5 for about 6.5 mA average / 13.5 mA peak; trim pfet pins use single cuts (about 2-4 mA DC per leg) and need EM review.
  - Decide on the TX termination trim.
| LO20 | **QVCO r6b + loop r5 (LO owner, round 5, 2026-11-12). Ports unchanged.** (1) `pt_lc_qvco5` r6b: tail 12 mA per core (was 8; reference diode 1.44/1 um) → QVCO supply 22–25 mA (was 17–18); tail gates now on an internal RC-filtered node (4 MΩ ppolyf + ~27 pF MOS + tail gates, pole ~1 kHz): **EN-to-ready 1.5 ms** (TAILG within 0.5 % in 1.0 ms at ss) — the LO sequencer must wait 1.5 ms after QVCO `EN` before band search; keep `EN` high across hops/TX-RX turnarounds. Layout bbox grows ~+66 µm in height (aux 621 × 223 µm under the core). (2) **Band-search policy:** preset VTUNE = 2.1 V (not VDD/2) during the band search, so the locked VTUNE sits in 1.8–2.4 V (high-Kvco half, Kvco bounded 80–200 MHz/V). (3) **LO18 rule update:** ICP code = 2-bit {25,50,75,100} µA nearest in log ratio to 100 µA × min(1, 90 MHz/V ÷ Kvco_measured). (4) `lo_lf` r5 published: R 10.38 k, C1 5.45 pF, C2 489.6 pF, R3 10 k, C3 8.84 pF; 405 × 481 µm (re-place). (5) **QVCO supply noise demand:** pushing −60 MHz/V (extracted, worst tank) ⇒ QVCO `VDD` noise ≤ 7 nV/√Hz at 100 kHz–1 MHz offsets (tones: **corrected 2026-11-13, see LO21: the limit is −60 dBc per supply tone and ≤ 0.2 ps rms in total, i.e. ≤ 33 µV pk at 1 MHz, 2.6 mV pk at 78.125 MHz; the earlier "−80 dBc" did not match the 30 µV figure and was not derived from a requirement**); needs a dedicated low-noise LDO/RC-filtered rail. (6) Fractional-N: acquire in integer mode, enable MASH after lock (ladder fork finding). | digital (FSM preset 2.1 V, EN wait 1.5 ms, ICP rule, MASH enable after lock), power/assembly (QVCO rail noise + current), floorplan (QVCO and lo_lf outlines) | Implement (1)–(3),(6) in the LO FSM; power owner: confirm a ≤ 7 nV/√Hz QVCO rail or tell the LO owner. |

  - (H1) The ss 3.24 pass is knife-edge on the 1 kohm CMD stand-in: 1.2 kohm gives 19 errors. The real NAND pull-up is estimated at 1.3-1.6 kohm at ss. tr7 has not been run from the real pt_wiredtx.
  - (H2) Pulse-fidelity +/-25 ps was demoted to "advisory" after results (coordinator decision, but it must be recorded as a dated decision).
  - (H3) The low-R swing fails.
  - (M) The 0.6x pad is a netlist scale, not a re-layout. pessimize() skips the pad's 6 V devices. fs/sf, ss/3.35, ff/3.15 and trim-code +/-1 are missing. No far-end eye limit is declared. No supply impedance in any bench.

  - Conditions for GO are listed in REVIEW.md.

### WT24 r7 (wired TX owner, now also owns the WT24 pre-driver; 2026-11-13): recorded decisions and declared limits (written BEFORE any r7 result)
- **Decision (coordinator, 2026-11-12), recorded here dated:** the isolated pulse-width check (|W_out − W_in| ≤ 25 ps, WT24 r4/r5) is **advisory only if the 3.24 Gb/s package eye passes with margin**. If the 3.24 package eye does not pass with margin, the pulse-width check is a FAIL again. (Answers REVIEW.md finding 2: the demotion is a coordinator decision of 2026-11-12, not a post-result relaxation by the cell owner.)
- **Decision (coordinator, 2026-11-12), recorded here dated:** the low-R swing failure (res_ff, 0.361–0.387 V pk vs the 0.40 V floor, REVIEW finding 3) is **closed by the 2-bit load trim** of the pre-driver: the trim must be shown to reach the 0.40 V floor at res_ff (calibrated code) in the r7 benches; the floor is not lowered.
- **Declared far-end eye limit (REVIEW finding 7), fixed before any r7 run:** at FAR_P − FAR_N (P4 package chain, 50 Ω ends): **0 errors AND inner eye height ≥ 200 mVppd AND eye width ≥ 0.45 UI.** Derivation: PCIe 1.x RX sensitivity 175 mVppd and RX eye 0.40 UI; +25 mV and +0.05 UI allocated to noise, crosstalk, supply bounce and random jitter that the bench does not contain. SATA RX levels (275–325 mVppd) are protocol amplitude settings (MAIN code), checked as swing, not by this limit. Bench: `scratch/wired_tx/s10/wt24/bench4.py` (real extracted core → CMD stage → route → extracted pre-driver → pad → package).
- **Bench conventions (r7):** the bond wire uses the pessimistic design-assumptions value (1.0 nH/mm, +0.5 mm, row 54) instead of 0.832 nH/mm; pessimize covers the pad's nfet/pfet_06v0 too (Rg 200 Ω per 10 µm, +25 % device C with Cgg 2.1 fF/µm at L = 0.7 µm — new design-assumptions entry), extracted C +15 % (low bound −15 %, no Rg). CMD route stand-in 50 Ω + 100 fF per leg (assumption until the floorplan route is extracted).

Report: `scratch/reviews/integration-m1.md`; model: `scratch/integration/m1-pdn/`. Three values separated by slashes are optimistic / nominal / pessimistic. No design file changed; risk rows are proposed in the report, section 8.

With the v6 block-to-switch distances (0.6–4.1 mm) the rf / wired / pll taps are at 137–139 / 274–277 / 488–490 mV. | assembly, island_support | Grid sheet ≤ 0.25 Ω/sq per net (≈ 25 % top-metal coverage), each island's loads abutting its switch, island strap ≤ 38 mV at the island current (rfdriver: ≤ 0.6 Ω). Extract and rerun `e1_static.py` on the v7 grid. |
| INT1-2 | **Island and mode currents.** rf island 99.1 mA in rf_tx (rfdriver 61.7 + warm LNA) against a 55 mA switch; pll island 103 mA with the measured LO chain (QVCO 25 + lo_mmd 34 + lo_ext 16 + polyphase 2.8 mA; 30.8 mA allocated) against 60 mA; wired island 110 mA with `pt_wrx_cap` 46.6 mA extracted (in no budget or island list) against 80 mA. Mode totals 344–351 mA against the 292 / 240 mA ceilings. | spec (power budget), island_support, wired_rx, timing (LO), rf_tx | Spec: add wrx_cap and the LO chain to `analog-architecture.json` and rerun `power_budget.py`. island_support: resize or split the switches. rf_tx: state how much rfdriver current comes through the RF_TX pad feed and how much from the island rail. |

With 3 nF at the macro the macro rail moves 68 / 219 / 361 mV pk-pk; with 6 nF 57 / 134 / 170; with 8 nF 45 / 95 / 123. The same decap spread over the analog core is 1.3–2× worse at the macro. Measured fillcap is 0.541 fF/µm² (≈ 0.45 nF in the macro), not the 1–3 nF of AP4-8 / PDN-02. | digital, assembly, decap | Digital: per-clock-domain power and the current pulse shape; a ripple limit for STA (proposed ≤ 150 mV pk-pk with a matching derate); cut the 78.125 MHz charge to ≤ 0.35 nC or accept ≥ 6 nF at the macro. With 1.5 nF MOS added at the macro: 0.87 / 0.93 / 0.95 Ω, 67 / 235 / 437 mV. | assembly, digital | Keep AP4-9 as the analog-side supplement; add ≥ 1.5 nF MOS at the macro before the 0.69 mm² dedicated area is released. |

Q is 0.9–2.9 in every plan, so PDN-03's damping condition (Q > 3) is not triggered while at least half the decap is MOS. | assembly, decap | Stay at 2–4 nF or ≥ 8 nF total unless the clock lines move; rerun `e2_impedance.py` on the final decap list. |

Two added pairs there bring the worst bond to 63 mA. Without the 8 strip capacitors every victim figure is 1.2–1.5× worse. Pads: confirm the ring rails through each custom family. |
| LO21 | **QVCO supply limit, corrected (answers INT1-6; LO owner, 2026-11-13).** LO20 item 5 was inconsistent: 30 µV at 1 MHz with −60 MHz/V is −61 dBc, not −80 dBc. −80 dBc came from no requirement. Derived limits: (a) RF-LO-JIT: the synthesizer is at 0.80 ps worst against 0.923 ps, so everything from the supply may add **≤ 0.2 ps rms in total** (one tone at S dBc adds √2·10^(S/20)/(2π·f_LO): −60 dBc = 0.09 ps, −55 = 0.16, −50 = 0.29 ps); (b) RF-RX-LOPN turned into discrete tones over the channel bandwidth: −47 dBc at 3 MHz, −42…−45 at 20 MHz, −57 at ≥ 100 MHz; (c) TX restricted-band emission (2483.5–2500 MHz, a 78.125 MHz sideband of a 2.42 GHz carrier at +8 dBm): −49 dBc. **Limit that holds: each supply-borne tone ≤ −60 dBc and the total ≤ 0.2 ps rms; random noise ≤ 7 nV/√Hz at 100 kHz–1 MHz (unchanged).** Allowed tone at the QVCO `VDD` pin (peak) = 2·f_m·10⁻³/60 MHz/V: **33 µV at 1 MHz, 0.65 mV at 20 MHz, 2.6 mV at 78.125 MHz, 5.2 mV at 156.25 MHz**. Against the INT1-6 ripple this needs **16 / 29 / 39 dB at 1 MHz (TX envelope; the pessimistic value rests on an assumed 30 % envelope current) and 16 / 23 / 30 dB at 78–156 MHz**; with several lines present the 0.2 ps sum is the tighter condition, so the regulator target is **≥ 45 dB at 1–20 MHz and ≥ 35 dB at 78–156 MHz**, plus a Kelvin ground. **Corrected after review (2026-11-13):** the loop does not relax the tone limit below 1 MHz. Supply-to-output pulling through the loop is K·v/f_m above fc and stays near K·v/fc down to the filter zero (~30 kHz), so the allowed tone at **30 kHz–1 MHz is a few µV peak (≈ 5–10 µV at fc 170–390 kHz for −60 dBc)**, the tightest region, and the regulator needs its ≥ 45 dB there too (board switching regulators and burst envelopes sit in this range). Not bounded by this row: AM conversion of 78–156 MHz ripple and random noise above 1 MHz. | power delivery, assembly, rf_tx | Power: build to these numbers. rf_tx: measure the rfdriver supply current against the envelope (it sets the 1 MHz number). |
| LO22 | **QVCO regulator: the ring regulator does not qualify; no QVCO regulator exists yet (answers INT1-6; LO owner, 2026-11-13).** Published extracted `pt_pll_vreg` (read-only use) with the QVCO load, VIN 3.15 V, tt / ss / ff (`scratch/lo-ap4/vreg/q.json`): one cell at 25 mA: rejection **16.7 / 19.1 / 17.9 dB at 1 MHz, 17.4 / 21.2 / 16.8 dB at 78 MHz**, VOUT 2.73 / 2.48 / 2.83 V; three cells in parallel (0.72 mm²): 22.5 / 21.0 / 26.5 dB at 1 MHz, 22.4 / 23.9 / 23.0 dB at 78 MHz, VOUT 2.81 / 2.66 / 2.83 V; 0.5–1 dB at 10 kHz and 4.5–6 dB at 100 kHz in every case (its reference is 0.9 × VIN by design). Against LO21 (≥ 45 dB at 1–20 MHz, ≥ 35 dB at 78–156 MHz) a single native follower misses by 20 dB or more, so a variant of this cell is not the answer. **What is needed (not built):** two cascaded rejection stages (cascoded native-NMOS follower, or follower + pre-filter) with the gate referred to an RC-filtered bandgap-derived level, 25 mA, dropout ≈ 0.45–0.6 V, i.e. **VDD_LO ≈ 2.55–2.75 V**. The QVCO has only been verified at 3.15–3.35 V (start-up at the weakest tank is 0.91–1.24 V differential at 3.15 V), so it must be re-verified, and probably re-biased, at 2.55 V before the regulator can be sized. Until both exist the on-chip LO fails LO21 on any shared rail; the external-LO baseline does not use the QVCO and is not affected. | power delivery, coordinator | Decide who builds the regulator (LO owner can, as its own task: QVCO at 2.55–2.75 V first, then regulator design, layout, extracted PSRR vs frequency). |
| LO23 | **LO chain currents per block (answers INT1-2, for the power-delivery owner).** Extracted, ss…ff, enabled: `pt_lc_qvco5` 22–25 mA (12 mA tail per core + bias; quiet rail); `pt_lo_mmd` 17.3–34.8 mA (ss/32/3.15 … ff/22/3.35; 24–26 mA at tt); `pt_lo_ext` internal or external-CML mode 11.8 mA at tt (INT1-2 uses 16 mA: take 16 mA as the ff bound until the 12-corner rerun is in), passive external mode 0.19 mA; `pt_lo_obs` 5.8–8.0 mA when enabled and dividing (ss…ff; the 2.9–3.0 mA first written here was the current of the four ss / 3.15 V corner runs in which the published cell does not divide, an open failure of `pt_lo_obs`), 1.4–2.8 µA off (13.9 µA in one nominal run); `pt_lo_mmd_qdum` and `pt_lo_pfdcp` **never measured** (by design ≈ 8 mA and ≈ 1 mA). `pt_polyphase` (2.8 mA in INT1-2) must not be powered or connected (LO-INTEGRATION.md section 1). **On-chip LO running: ≈ 66–93 mA (two of the six terms are estimates), of which 23–26 mA on the quiet rail. External-LO baseline (die 1): lo_ext 12–16 mA only**, QVCO / MMD / PFD off. The 30.8 mA allocation covers the external baseline, not the synthesizer. | spec (power budget), power delivery, island_support | Budget both modes; size the LO supply for ≥ 100 mA or split quiet / digital-like LO rails as in LO-INTEGRATION.md section 3. |
| LO24 | **Lock time is set by the loop-filter RC corner (open item "50.6 vs 50 µs"; LO owner, 2026-11-13).** Behavioural cycle-accurate loop (`run_loop.hop4`) on the published r5 filter with the laid-out C2 (`scratch/lo-ap4/lock/lock_rc.json`): t_lock(2°) = 5.9–7.3 × R·C2, nearly independent of Icp (75–150 µA: 39.9–40.5 µs) and weakly dependent on the band-search residual (0.3 → 0.1 V: 40.5 → 35.1 µs). Nominal 40.5 µs; over the loop grid's own filter ranges (R ±25 %, C2 ±15 %): **21–60 µs at Kv 80 / N 56 and up to 64.5 µs at Kv 200 / N 44**. The transistor-level 50.6–52.0 µs at ss is consistent with this effect (R·C2 ≈ +20…25 % at ss is inferred from the ratio, `lfcheck.json` supports about +20 %; it was not measured directly, and model-to-transistor agreement is about ±5 µs), so the miss is not "narrow": the slow filter corner misses the 50 µs limit by up to 15 µs. Fix candidate (not built): a 2-bit trim of the main filter R (×1.0 / 0.9 / 0.8 / 0.7) set from the measured RC corner (`pt_aosc` period against the reference): at R ×1.25, C2 ×1.15 the trim ×0.8 gives 47.4 µs (Kv 80) and ×0.7 gives 45.2 µs (Kv 200), PM 60–64°. Alternative: shorten the band search (digital) by 15 µs. | digital (IX-5 budget, 2 trim bits), LO | LO owner: `lo_lf` netlist + layout + ladder rerun as a separate task; until then IX-5 holds only for R·C2 ≤ 1.2 × nominal. |
| LO25 | **Band-search preset mismatch (found 2026-11-13).** LO20 item 2 and the lock ladder use a VTUNE preset of 2.1 V; the published `pt_lo_pfdcp` presets to VDD/2 (two 40 kΩ, `lo_pfdcp.spice:135-138`). With the published cell the loop locks in the low-Kvco half (25–40 MHz/V), outside the range the jitter grid and the ladder covered. ### Wired TX owner resume (session 11, 2026-11-13): mode-walk rows MW-15/16/17/24/31/35, exact pin list in `analog/wired_tx/INTEGRATION.md`

Applies to the PUBLISHED `pt_wiredtx` (`analog/wired_tx/wiredtx.spice`, quarter-rate core `wtx_serq4c`, 4 phases, line ≤ 2.5 Gb/s). The eighth-rate version (8 phases) is not published; its pin delta is listed in INTEGRATION.md section 7 and gets its own row when it is.

| ID | Change | Affects | Ask |
|---|---|---|---|
| WT25 | **Real serializer enable / clock / reset interface (answers MW-15; replaces the `pt_clocktree_slow` serializer model).** (1) **Clock in:** four free-running CMOS ring phases `WTX_PH0/90/180/270` (= `pll_tx.PH0/PH2/PH4/PH6`, ring = core rate / 4, 360–625 MHz). They may run or be static at any level while `SER_EN` = 0. There is no bit clock, no `word_load`, no `bit_phase` input: the block owns its /5 (`ct_div5`) and /2 (`wtx_w40`) dividers. (2) **Enable = reset:** `SER_EN` is one static, asynchronous level. 0: core phases gated inside the core, W40 toggle flop held in reset, `WORD_CLK` / `WORD_CLK40` low, CMD outputs low (both legs off). 1: counters run. The /5 counter has no reset pin (self-starting); the first `WORD_CLK40` period after the rise may be short (measured on the eighth-rate core: 9.85 instead of 12.35 ns; the published block's steady period is 16 ns at 2.5 Gb/s and its first period was not measured), so the relation of `WORD_CLK40` to `SER_EN` is arbitrary within 5 register periods. (3) **Required source:** `SER_EN = (wiredtx.state.state == active) AND island_on[wired] AND tx_ring_ready AND NOT debug.wtx_ext.enable`, where `tx_ring_ready` = TX ring enabled for ≥ its lock time (NOT implementable yet: timing must state the lock time at ss; alternative = the frequency counter on `pll_tx.FBCK` within a tolerance the digital owner sets, e.g. ± 0.5 %; do not use the analog `LOCK` pin, timing README: wrong at the top of the range). `SER_EN` must go low before `MODE_H/MODE_F`, the ring band / modulus or the reference change, and whenever the ring is disabled. This is `en_eff[2]` with two more terms; `ct_ser_run` must NOT drive it (it can never assert and never clears). (4) **Clock out:** `WORD_CLK40` (core rate / 40, 50 % duty, ≤ 62.5 MHz; ≤ 81 MHz in the eighth-rate version) is the ONLY clock of the `wire_tx_clk` domain: bind `wiredtx.WORD_CLK40` → wired iso clamp → `digital.wire_tx_clk`. `WORD_CLK` (core / 20) is for observation only (`WCLK_OBS` / frequency counter); it must not share a net with `wordclk.WORD_CLK` or the macro's `ct_word_clk` output. (5) **Data in:** D0..D39 per WT19 / WT20 (unchanged): 40 core bits per `WORD_CLK40`, D0 first on the line; D0..D19 from falling-edge flops valid ≤ 2.0 ns after the fall, D20..D39 from rising-edge flops valid ≤ 3.5 ns after the rise; these delays INCLUDE the iso clamp, the macro clock insertion and both routes (mode-walk U3: not yet in the SDC). The first 4 words after `SER_EN` rises are not guaranteed on the line: send idle / training there. (6) **No request / ready:** the block takes a word on every `WORD_CLK40` cycle and has no back-pressure pin. `wire_tx_request` has no analog source: tie it to 1 in the macro instance (word stage loads every cycle). **Demand on digital:** with the request tied high an underrun today re-sends the stale egress head; the word stage must substitute an idle / fill word whenever no new word is valid. Underrun cannot be handled by stalling. (7) **Multi-chip launch (`ct_word_load`, `ct_bit_phase`, `ct_qualified`):** no analog consumer. Lane-to-lane alignment of several chips is frequency lock to a shared reference plus an arbitrary word phase per chip (up to 40 core bits); HDMI/DP receivers de-skew lanes on control characters, so this is accepted; a deterministic launch would need a `SER_EN` release synchronised to the reference in each chip and gives ± 1 register period at best. | digital (SER_EN source, `wire_tx_clk`, `wire_tx_request` tie, retire `ct_ser_run` / `ct_word_load` / `ct_bit_phase` as serializer controls, SDC with the clamp and route), chip-top (bindings) | Digital: implement (3), (4), (6); delete or re-purpose the launch logic. Chip-top: bind per INTEGRATION.md. |
| WT26 | **TX ring `pll_tx` (`pt_pll_ring_core`): instance, enable, band, modulus, reference (answers MW-16).** Wired TX needs, per mode: ring frequency = core rate / 4 = 360–625 MHz (TMDS 1.485 → 371.25; SATA 1.5 → 375; DP RBR 1.62 → 405; PCIe 2.5 → 625; ×2 / ×4 rates use 393–625). (1) **Instance:** one `pt_pll_ring_core`, `VDD` = `pll_vreg2.VOUT_TX`, `PH0/2/4/6` → `wiredtx.WTX_PH0/90/180/270` (direct, matched routes, no clockmux: the clockmux has one single-ended output and cannot carry phases); `PH1/3/5/7` loaded with matched dummies today (they become live in the eighth-rate version). (2) **One enable definition:** `pll_tx.EN = wire_txpll_en AND island_on[pll] AND vreg_tx_ok`, with `wire_txpll_en` = `ring_l.config.enable` (already in the digital agent's working tree, `pt_regmap_defs.svh` RING_L_CONFIG); `rfpll.state` (en_eff[11]) is the LO synthesiser's and must not be reused. (3) **Band:** `ring_l.config.band` (reset 1 = high band) → `pll_tx.BAND`. Low band (0) is required for ring ≤ ~480 MHz, i.e. TMDS 1.485, SATA 1.5, DP RBR 1.62 at ×1 and every ×1 rate of the eighth-rate core (ring 300–405). (4) **Modulus:** `ring_l.config.modulus[1:0]` (values 16 / 8 / 4) → `N8` = (modulus == 1), `N4` = (modulus == 2); the decode and the binding do not exist yet; `consolidation.pll.feedback_n` is superseded. `HOLD` = 0 for the TX ring (tie low). (5) **Reference:** ref = ring / N, N ∈ {4, 8, 16}: e.g. 625 MHz → 39.0625 (N16) / 78.125 (N8) / 156.25 (N4); 371.25 → 23.203 / 46.406 / 92.81; 405 → 25.3125 / 50.625 / 101.25. A 27 MHz or pixel-clock reference cannot produce these without a reference divider, which does not exist on the die (dropped field `refinput.select.reference_divider`): board spec must list one reference per rate family, or timing adds an R divider. (6) **Bias:** `pll_tx.IBIAS` ← 10 µA sourced from the pll bias bank (`BIAS_pll_ring_tx`), enabled with `pll_tx.EN`. (7) `FBCK` → `fc_src[0]`, `LOCK` → status only. | digital (enable equation, modulus decode, bindings of the existing `ring_l.config.*` fields), timing (R divider or none; phase skew, see WT29), chip-top (instance + bindings), board spec (reference list) | Digital: decode + bind. Timing: answer (5). |
| WT27 | **TX pad TMDS branch tie-off (answers MW-17a).** `pt_wiredtx` has no `CMD_TMDS_*`, `TMDS_BIAS`, `EN_TMDS`, `TMDS_CODE*` (WT22). Until pads remove the branch from `pad_txpad` (AP4-2 / X5 on the pad side, −0.04 mm² and less pad C), chip-top must tie the pad pins `TMDS_P`, `TMDS_N`, `TMDS_BIAS` to VSS (switch gates and the 16 × 20/1 tail gate off; no static current). `netlist.py:106-107,135-136` rules for `TX_TMDS_*` are to be deleted. `tmds.config.current_code`, `tmds.state`, `tmds.idle` have no consumer: TMDS mode = `EN_MAIN` with `wiredtx.config.current_code` (102 → 8.4 mA, 125 → 10.3 mA), pad termination off, 50 Ω sink pull-ups. The pads `interlock` output `EN_TMDS` is left unconnected and its `REQ_TMDS` tied low. | chip-top (tie-off, rule removal), pads (remove the branch: X5 area), digital (drop or alias `tmds.*`) | Pads: remove the TMDS legs from `wired-transmit` when the 0.6× switch resize (WT24 r6) is laid out; one re-extraction for both. |
| WT28 | **WTX_EXT fallback fixture rebuilt for the post-X5 block (answers MW-17b / mode-walk F4a,b).** `integration/wtx_fallback.py`: external-route request of the published control cell (pin still named `TMDS_REQUEST`, decode `txpad.config.branch == 2`) now goes to the interlock `REQ_MAIN` (was `REQ_TMDS`); bias bank `EN2` = `EN_MAIN`; `L_CODE0..7` → `MAIN_CODE0..7`; `POST_POL` = 0, D0..D39 = 0; steering buffers only on MAIN / POST; pad `TMDS_P/N/TMDS_BIAS` = VSS. `verification/check_chip_fallback.py`: the code register is `wiredtx.config.current_code` (was `tmds.config.current_code`), probes `EN_MAIN` / `MAIN_BIAS`. Check: `wtx_fallback.extension()` now builds against `generated/wiredtx.ext.spice` (before: `ValueError` pin mismatch `TMDS_CODE0..7`). **Not rerun:** the live SPI co-simulation (`check_chip_fallback.py`, hours) and `check_chip_fallback_layout.py`; the physical bring-up routing (`analog/bringup_control`) must take `TMDS_REQUEST` to `REQ_MAIN`, and `bringup_control/generated/evidence.json` hashes `integration/wtx_fallback.py`, so `check_chip_fallback_layout.py` will report stale control-macro evidence until `bringup_control/build.py` is rerun. The request is still TMDS-shaped (`branch == 2`, `stage == tmds`, state active, `idle == 0`; alias `tmds_code`). In the full chip `REQ_MAIN` then has two sources (this request and the normal-mode request): a combiner on the wired rail is undefined (INTEGRATION.md section 4). **F4c (chip netlist):** bind `rx_wtx_ext.OUTN` (legs swapped at the receiver, as `analog/wired_tx/wtx_ext_rx.spice`) → debug→core iso clamp → core→wired iso clamp → `wiredtx.DX`; `wiredtx.WTX_EXT_DATA` is an OUTPUT (→ wired iso clamp → status bit), it must not share the receiver's net. **F4d (receiver fails from 0.5 Gb/s, TIM-08):** unchanged, timing's; the fallback is usable only for slow patterns until it is fixed. | chip-top / assembly (bindings), bring-up control owner (routing), timing (TIM-08) | Verification owner: rerun the live fallback check when a slot is free. |
| WT30 | **Electrical idle, repetition, signal detect (answers MW-35, TX side; decision per protocol).** Published `pt_wiredtx` has no idle input; `digital.wire_idle` has no consumer and `wiredtx.idle.idle` is consumed only by the fallback control cell (`L_IDLE`). (a) **PCIe / SATA / DP (AC-coupled) electrical idle and SATA OOB:** need differential 0 with the common mode held, entry / exit ≤ 1 word (OOB burst 106.7 ns = 6–8 `WORD_CLK40` periods). Dropping `EN_MAIN` does give differential 0 but steps the common mode by I × 25 Ω (0.2–0.5 V) and goes through the interlock and bias switch (settling not measured): usable as power-down only. **Planned in the block (with the next republish, not built):** pin `IDLE` on the CMD stage: both pad switch gates of a branch driven to the same ON level, so the tail current splits equally (differential 0, common mode unchanged). Source: `digital.wire_idle` re-timed by the digital in the `wire_tx_clk` domain; the pin is asynchronous. (b) **USB:** idle = SE0 / J via the FS driver and HS current steered off: see the USB rows. (c) **TMDS / 100BASE-TX:** no electrical idle. (d) **Repetition ×2 / ×4 (×8):** the digital's (WT19), not built in the RTL: 1.25 Gb/s, 0.48 Gb/s and every rate below 1.44 Gb/s depend on it or on the FPGA pre-repeating. ### TOP (chip-top integration owner, 2026-11-13): the one chip-level connectivity and what each owner must change

Source of truth: `projects/programmable_transceiver_platform/integration/top/pt_chip.conn` (every instance, every pin bound to a named net, pin lists read from each block's published subckt), with `masters.txt`, `ctl_ports.txt`, `decisions.md`. Regression: `integration/top/run.sh` (connectivity checks with must-fail self-tests, register-map cross-derivation, macro port diff, the three mode-walk self-tests, and a stub-level SPI -> register -> analog-pin sim with a negative control). `verification/chip_assembly/netlist.py` PATHS/ENABLE_FIELD rules are superseded by this file and must not be used as connectivity intent any more.

| ID | Change | Owner | Ask |
|---|---|---|---|
| TOP-1 | **Boundary rule (coordinator decisions applied).** The digital macro presents one named port per analog control pin group (`c_*`, 214 ports / 522 bits today), already decoded (one-hot where the cell is one-hot), at the cell's width and safe reset; `analog_cfg[578:0]`, `debug_cfg[255:0]` and `en_eff[19:0]` no longer cross the boundary. Ports of a switched island read 0 while the island is off (existing regfile gating). Generated black box: `integration/top/generated/pt_analog_top.sv` (863 port bits); structural netlist `generated/pt_analog_top.spice`. | digital | Add the ports of `ctl_ports.txt`; rows TOP-10.. list every port that needs a new field or a decode. The regression's `macro` and `cfg` counts go to 0 when done; then compile `sim/tb_top_boundary.sv` with `+define+PT_MACRO_HAS_CTL`. |

| AP4-4a | **Converters repack + single-ended clock: candidate STAGED, not published (2026-11-13, converters).** One new layout per block; the repack and the MW-3 clock change are in the same layout (`CONV_AP4=1`; `scratch/converters-ap4/stage4/<block>/generated/`). Measured GDS outlines: **adc_i/adc_q 465.46 × 1241.16 → 480.28 × 936.30 µm (0.5777 → 0.4497 mm², −0.128 each)**; **dac_i/dac_q 674.50 × 275.93 → 668.50 × 200.63 µm (0.1861 → 0.1341 mm², −0.052 each)**; total −0.360 mm² against the 0.61 asked. The pin is gated by the block's enable on the block. **ADC** (`CLK` = `clockmux.CONV_CLK`): on-block one-shot opens the acquisition window on the rising edge; new static pins `ACQ0/ACQ1` (from the existing `adc_*.config.acquisition_ns[1:0]`): 0 nominal (5.0 ns tt, 6.1 ss, 4.4 ff, schematic), 1 short, 2 long, 3 debug (window = clock high time; does not fit 40 MS/s at ss). New output `PH` = interleave phase (0: next edge starts core A), so the digital side selects A/B by `PH` and never by edge counting. **DAC** (`CLK` = `rf_tx_clk_out` per DT-6): captures on the FALLING edge on the block; the top must NOT swap or invert. **Handshake:** converters produce no clocks; capture rule, `rf_rx_valid`, `rf_tx_request` tie-high, "discard 4 samples after enable / clock start" and the DAC `HOLD_ZERO` enable rule are in `analog/converters/INTEGRATION.md` §1–2. **Needed from others:** digital: route `acquisition_ns[1:0]` → `ACQ1:0` of both ADCs, add `adc_*.config.interleave` → `ILEN`, take `PH` + `CODE`/`CODEB` and implement the A/B register and valid; assembly: bind `CONV_CLK` → ADC `CLK`, `rf_tx_clk_out` → DAC `CLK`, bind `PH`, drop the `CLKP/CLKN` rules; timing: `CONV_CLK` ≤ 40 MHz, duty 40–60 %; a second converter clock is needed for 80 MS/s TX with a 40 MS/s ADC. | WT29 | **MW-31 measured (single points, CMD level, nominal extraction): the published `pt_wiredtx` fails at 2.5 Gb/s at ss/32/3.15 with the real TX ring as phase source, and the cause is the ring's phase DUTY CYCLE.** Bench (`analog/wired_tx/sim_ringsrc.py`, `sim_realring.py`; raw `scratch/wired_tx/s11/blk`, `tune`; record `analog/wired_tx/generated/wiredtx-realring.json`): extracted `pt_pll_ring_core` open loop (VCTRL forced on `ATB_VCTRL`, REF low, BAND = 1) on the regulated supply → 30 Ω / 60 fF route per phase → extracted `pt_wiredtx` (`PH0/2/4/6`), CMD outputs decoded on a fixed grid, launch as the block envelope, about 100 bits per run. **Runs at ss/32/3.15 V, 626 MHz (2.505 Gb/s):** (1) real ring, ring supply 2.69 V: **11 errors / 101 bits**, jitter 322 ps pp, eye 77 ps of 399. Isolating controls with ideal sources, one real-ring property at a time: (2) full rail, 50 % duty, ideal spacing: 0 errors, eye 240 ps (the published envelope number); (3) swing 2.69 V only: 0 errors, eye 306 ps; (4) swing 2.69 V + measured rise spacing (0 / −35 / −29 / −38 ps): 0 errors, eye 323 ps; (5) swing 2.69 V + duty 0.575 at mid-swing, ideal spacing: **11 errors / 101, eye 121 ps, the same postcursor mismatches as the real ring.** So reduced swing and spacing are not the cause at this point; duty is. Mechanism: the quarter-rate core closes each window on the FALLING edge of the previous phase (`S k = QW k & QW k−1`); +7.5 % duty is +120 ps of window overlap on top of the tuned 0.16 UI. Second ss supply point, ring 2.57 V (low end of the regulator range; duty 0.58–0.59): 11 errors / 100, eye 55 ps. **Other points:** tt/27/3.3 V, ring supply 2.925 V (duty 0.52 at ring mid-rail, 0.50 at the serializer threshold): 0 errors / 101, eye 226 ps on the fixed grid; this is one point, not a qualification. ss/32/3.15, 1.62 Gb/s (ring 405 MHz, low band, duty 0.53): 0 errors / 100, eye 364 ps of 617. **Ring alone (ss/32, 2.69 V, 60 fF per phase):** duty at ring mid-rail 0.57–0.58 at 625 MHz, 0.53 at 405 MHz, 0.63 at 824 MHz (tt 2.925 V 625 MHz: 0.52); rise spacing of PH0/2/4/6 365 / 406 / 390 / 438 ps (tt 381 / 402 / 393 / 423): every phase is early relative to PH0, i.e. **PH0 is about 30–35 ps late** (the divider tap `DKB` sits on the phase-buffer input I0; independent review), and PH7 is a further 60–68 ps early with 50 mV less swing. **Limits of this evidence (independent review, NO-GO as a qualification):** one ss supply point (the regulator gives 2.57–2.95 V at ss over VIN and load, and its reset default is bypass, ring on the raw rail: not run); corner and ring supply changed together between the ss and tt runs; no ff / sf / fs; no pessimistic values on block or ring; 101 bits; CMD pins only (the published steering buffers do not pass 2.5 Gb/s anyway, WT24); the open-loop ring is still settling during the run (ss 624.9 → 626.9 MHz), which inflates real-ring jitter on the fixed decode grid (ss eye 77 → 84 ps, tt 226 → 254 ps on the measured mean rate); no kill-phase negative control on the block (the ideal controls show the bench can pass, runs (1) and (5) that it can fail). **No fix is in the published block.** Fix: (a) wired TX: close each window on the RISING edge of the next phase (`S k = QW k & !QW k+1`, as `wtx_serq8` does): a core-macro change with re-layout and a new envelope; (b) timing: `ct_phbuf8` duty within 50 ± 2 % at ss/ff on the regulated supply, and the PH0 / PH7 skews, which matter for the eighth-rate core (all eight phases) even after (a). | timing / wired RX (ring owner: `ct_phbuf8` duty; PH0 tap and PH7 skew), wired TX (window logic), chip-top (phase routes) | Timing: confirm duty and spacing on your bench and state what the buffer can reach. Needed before the top rate is called proven or failed at ss: ring supply 2.57 V / 2.9 V / bypass, tt at 3.15 V, ff / sf / fs, pessimistic values, ≥ 254 bits, line-level result. |
| WT31 | **2.7–3.24 Gb/s TX (MW-24): what exists, measured against real neighbours; NOT closed.** Pieces: eighth-rate CMOS core `wtx_serq8` (`serq8_macro.py`, `generated/fast/wtx_serq8.*`, 767.6 × 458.8 µm, DRC 0, LVS unique, not in `pt_wiredtx`); pre-driver `wt24_tr7` (`scratch/pads-wt24/lay_tr7`, DRC 0, not published, review NO-GO); pad `wired-transmit` with the 0.6× switch as a netlist scale only. No CML mux exists. **Results, ss/32/3.15, 3.24 Gb/s:** (1) core alone, ideal phases, extracted: nominal 0 errors, eye 156 ps of 309; pessimistic (Rg, +25 % device C, +15 % wire C) 45 errors. Window widths and hop timing are correct at the pessimistic values (S k 0.95–1.13 slots, every F k stable ≥ 2 slots around its window); the eye closes in the 6-gate NAND-OR tree (half-tree outputs spread 0.5 slot). (2) REAL chain (`s10/wt24/bench4.py`, first ever run; two bench bugs fixed: unsaveable vector `v(a,b)`, and far-end scoring at 0 V although the 100 nF coupling keeps the start-up offset — `s11/rescore.py`): nominal extracted core → CMD stage (16/16, 24/24) → 50 Ω / 100 fF → extracted `tr7` → pad (Rg and +15 % wire C applied; the +25 % device C was NOT, see the pessimiser bug below, so this run is optimistic) → P4 package, pessimistic bond: **14 errors**, core eye 124 ps at Y, and the pad-gate differential shows 94 crossings where about 113 are expected (isolated bits lost in the pre-driver). Declared limit (WT24 r7): 0 errors, ≥ 200 mVppd, ≥ 0.45 UI: FAIL. The earlier "0 errors at 3.24" (r6) held only for an ideal 1 kΩ source. (3) Pre-driver + pad + package are not the limit: `tr7` schematic with the layout's wiring C and a clean full-rate source passes (0 errors, 0.50 V, 255 ps = 0.83 UI). **Conclusion:** a full-swing CMOS path that carries 309-ps pulses through 9 gates (tree 6, CMD 2, input inverter 1) does not close at ss; this is a topology limit of the present design, not a proven physical limit (no physical-limits entry). **Candidate that removes every 1-slot CMOS pulse (schematic pre-driver with wiring-C stand-ins, extracted pessimised pad + package, `scratch/wired_tx/s11/xor/`):** the pre-driver's pull-down pair becomes an XOR network (OUTN low when A ≠ B, OUTP low when A = B, 2-stack 72 µm nfets, same loads / trim), A and B being two half-rate NRZ streams offset by one slot that carry the running XOR of the data (precoding in the digital: one XOR per bit). **Idealised in every candidate run (read the numbers with this in mind):** ideal complementary sources (60-ps edges, no jitter) behind 1 kΩ; schematic pre-driver with wiring-C stand-ins; pad switch 0.6× as a netlist scale; ideal VDD, CASCODE, `TERM_VCM`, `MAIN_BIAS` (1.9 V); 120 scored bits; either output polarity accepted. Numbers below are AFTER a pessimiser fix found by the independent review (the +25 % device C of the pad had been written into the wrong subckt in `bench4.pess_all`, and is in `scratch/pads-wt24/bench.pessimize` still: every earlier WT24 "pad pessimised" result, r2–r6, lacks the pad's device-C adder; `s11/xor/summary_p2.json`). ss/32/3.15 pessimistic, far end, limit 0 errors / 0.20 V / 0.45 UI: control `tr7` schematic with a clean full-rate source 0.471 V / 248 ps (0.80 UI); XOR candidate res_ss 0.346 V / 213 ps (0.69 UI), res_ff trim 0 0.317 V / 235 ps, at 3.35 V 0.385 V / 223 ps, fs 0.551 V / 240 ps, sf 0.371 V / 218 ps; A–B skew +30 ps 0.328 V / 205 ps, −30 ps 0.250 V / 167 ps (0.54 UI), +60 ps 0.230 V / 157 ps (0.51 UI), **−60 ps 0.148 V / 105 ps: FAIL**; negative control on the same cell (B stuck) 27 errors; 14–16 mA. (Before the fix, with low-C `pess=False` at ff/22/3.35: 0.62–0.79 V / 263–273 ps; tt 0.463 V / 246 ps; 1.5 kΩ source 0.387 V / 221 ps; not rerun.) **What it does not solve:** the two streams must arrive within about −30 … +60 ps of relative skew including their jitter (the asymmetry comes from the stack order and is fixable; nothing between −30 and −60 ps was run). The serq8 2-slot window mux gives 218 ps pp at the pessimistic corner and 9–11 errors / 80 bits in its ×2 mode there (`s11/half/`, launch alignment of that bench not verified): unusable as the stream source. An 8-flop toggle-XOR tree in front (each flop toggles on one ring phase; XOR pairs 4 slots apart, then 2) gives 0 errors but 0.089 V / 111 ps (0.36 UI) at ss pessimistic with ideal flops: FAIL. (2) `usb_hs_drv` timing: the cell needs 1.7-3.3 ns from `usb_hs_drv` to the line and 2.6-3.5 ns to idle, the data path 1.3-2.0 ns; `usb_hs_drv` is word-granular (40-bit word = 10 USB bits at x4 repetition), so hold the serializer data at the K level before the packet and at the last EOP level after it; the HS EOP is stretched by 0-9 bit times unless the FPGA pads the SYNC (hubs do both; a SOF EOP is 40 bits). A bit-accurate idle needs a second serial lane (not built; say so if required). (3) `usb_tx_dp/dn/oe` from one clock edge, mutual skew <= 1 ns. (4) HS transmit needs `pt_wiredtx` MODE_F (x4), EN_MAIN = 1, EN_POST = 0, MAIN/POST tail codes 0 (WIRE_TX pad silent). | USB-D2 | **USB protocol split and turnaround (recorded decision found: `spec/block-diagram.md:33`, `spec/parallel-datapath-candidate.md:23-26`, `spec/streaming-transport-v2.md:191-206`: FPGA owns packets (SIE, NRZI, bit stuffing, CRC); the die does serialization, burst capture and timed line drive; USB uses the 100 MHz direct byte stream, modelled 342 ns reply).** So no protocol logic is needed on the die, and the digital agent's `rtl/pt_usb.sv` (raw line symbols, in progress, not yet in `integration/pt_chip.sv`, which still says the USB macros are unimplemented at line 1) is the right shape. **Reasoning on turnaround from the analog side:** the TX path adds <= 3.3 ns from `usb_hs_drv` / `usb_tx_oe` to the line, so the 192-bit (400 ns) HS limit and the FS device limit of 6.5 bit times (541 ns) are set entirely by the host-link round trip (342 ns modelled at full clock, about 2.4x slower at achieved clocks, DIG-03 open). If DIG-03 does not close, HS and FS device-role handshakes need an on-die armed reply (FPGA preloads the handshake, the die launches it on the received EOP); LS (6.5 bits = 4.3 us) and host role are safe either way. **Receive side still open for digital:** squelch (det_thr 2) and disconnect (det_thr 3) share one comparator pair, so the logic must switch the code (a host samples disconnect only in the SOF EOP) and respect the 100 ns `det_en` settling (DT7-1); `wire_status` bits `usb_squelch` / `detect_event` need a producer; HS receive needs the sampling-phase selection of mode-walk A3. | digital, FPGA reference design, coordinator | Coordinator: decide the armed-reply question together with DIG-03. |

The 14 x 20/1 pfet sources per leg need about 15 % more width or a lower threshold option to centre ss (the earlier 372-379 mV figure had no package or return resistance). (2) **HS_PBIAS is kicked 0.35 V peak-peak by every data transition** through the 28 source drains (the off leg's sources fall into triode at VDD), against the 2 kohm output of `pt_wtx_hsbias`: data-dependent amplitude 362-432 mV and 28 mV less eye. (4) FS rise/fall matching with an ideal driver is 1.10-1.15 tt, 1.27-1.33 ss, 0.95-1.00 ff (limit 0.90-1.11): the known USB-01 residual, unchanged by the pre-driver (delta <= 0.023); LS crossover at 50 pF with the D- pull-up is 2.00 V tt / 2.08 V ff with an ideal driver (limit 2.0 V), also pad-set. (5) The lifecycle bench's `receive_control_guard` (conflict and supply-valid gating of every control pin, `wired-receive-usb-lifecycle.spice:3178-3195`) is behavioural and has no hardware counterpart; `pt_usb_txdrv` covers HS-vs-FS and ISLAND_OFF for the six driver pins only; the rest is register interlocks (USB-D1). Assembly: USB_DP/USB_DN ports. |

| CP4-1 | **Island -> macro isolation moves into the macro.** 128-129 of the 147 `iso_clamp` / `iso_clamp_x4` cells feed only the macro (core VDD). Replace them by hand-instantiated standard-cell AND2 at the macro input pins, ISO = the macro's own `island_off[k]`. Stay outside: the 15 clamps with an island-rail receiver or a pad, and the clamps on `rf_rx_clk`, `host_word_clk`, `fc_src[0]` (and `fc_src[1]` if CP4-4 is taken), which also feed `clk_obs`. ### Integration checks M2 + M3: substrate coupling, oscillator pulling, LO leakage, spurs (integration analyst, 2026-11-13)

Report: `scratch/reviews/integration-m23.md`; models and sims: `scratch/integration/m23-substrate-pulling/`. Three values separated by slashes are optimistic / nominal / pessimistic. No design file changed; risk rows are proposed in the report, section 9. Distances are edge gaps unless stated.

| ID | Change | Affects | Ask |
|---|---|---|---|
| INT23-1 | **External-LO leakage through the bond wires fails RF-RX-LOLEAK at every bound.** REF_IN pair → RF_RX pair (0.89 mm apart in `bond_map_current`): −48.5 / −41 / −31 dBm at RF_RX against −57 dBm; self-mixing DC 15 / 38 / 113 mV of the ±250 mV coarse trim. AUX_CLK pair (1.74 mm): −69 / −60 / −46 dBm. Not a floorplan item. | pads, timing (lo_ext), rf_rx, spec, board | Figures are for 4 Vpp differential; at the 1.0 Vpp end of the `lo_mode` 3 window: −60.5 / −53 / −43 dBm. Decide one: (a) run the CML path at ≤ 0.63 Vpp (needs the window extended downward; ≤ 0.2 Vpp for the pessimistic bound); (b) REF_IN ≥ 1.7 mm from RF_RX in the bond map (≥ 3 mm for the pessimistic bound); (c) waive RF-RX-LOLEAK in the external-LO bench mode. rf_rx: budget up to 113 mV of LO-induced DC in the per-gain table. |
| INT23-2 | **QVCO position (PA pulling and LO leakage through the RF bonds).** The QVCO runs at the RF carrier, so the PA pulls it (LO-01 rule not followed). Bond-to-spiral coupling gives an EVM share of −69 / −57.5 / −32 dB at the v6 seed (limit −35 dB). | assembly, timing (LO) | Spiral centres ≥ 1.7 mm from the west die edge and ≥ 1.0 mm along the edge from both the RF_TX and RF_RX pad pairs (then ≥ +9 dB at the pessimistic bound; LO at RF_RX ≤ −72 dBm). Do not rotate a spiral axis toward the RF pads. LO owner: reopen LO-01, or state TX on the autonomous LO as OFDM-limited until measured. |
| INT23-3 | **QVCO keep-away from switching blocks (substrate).** Extracted QVCO: 18.4 MHz/V quasi-static on the substrate, 5.2 kHz of lock range per mV of tone near f0. Needed transfer from the macro for −80 dBc at 78.125 MHz: −27 / −37 / −42 dB. | assembly, timing (LO) | Digital macro ↔ QVCO ≥ 800 µm with a continuous tie sea between them (nominal, +7 dB); the pessimistic bound also needs the grounded backside or deep n-well under the QVCO NMOS. The 300 µm rule in v6 gives a −65 dBc spur at nominal. Rings and rfdriver ↔ QVCO ≥ 500 µm (keep). QVCO ↔ LNA ≥ 250 µm (keep). VTUNE filter capacitor C3 at the QVCO, not at the loop filter. |
| INT23-4 | **Digital macro ↔ bbfilter_i/q.** 125 / 179 µm in v6: transfer −7…−21 dB. In-band macro noise × PGA 42 dB reaches the ADC at +19 / −8 / −38 dB against 0.25 LSB. | assembly, rf_rx, digital | Assembly: bbfilter ≥ 500 µm from the macro with a tie sea, or deep n-well under the stage-1 and biquad NMOS with wide rings. rf_rx: substrate/ground rejection of stage 1 from the extracted netlist. Digital: the < 10 MHz content of the macro supply current (the 2 / 5 / 15 % used here is a guess). |
| INT23-5 | **D2H / analog_cfg lanes over or beside the converters.** Unshielded lanes inject 1.5 / 5 / 13 mA pk into the substrate: +14 / −15 / −48 dB at adc_i when routed over the ADCs, +22 / −8 / −42 dB at adc_q in the east corridor. XT-05's 20 µm is not enough. | assembly, digital | Lanes on Metal3 or above over a continuous Metal1/Metal2 shield tied to digital VSS, never over an ADC, bbfilter or refbuf; ≥ 100 µm from adc_i/adc_q/bbfilter edges with a p+ strap tied to the converter ground in between; internal lane edges ≥ 1 ns. |
| INT23-6 | **Digital macro ↔ dac_i/dac_q has no rule (22 µm in v6).** Transfer −3…−8 dB; TX spur at ±78.125 MHz in `tx_dac_direct`: +33 / +12.5 / −7 dB against −40 dBr with the AP4-9 decap, −21 dB with 0 nF. | assembly, converters | Add a rule: digital ↔ dac_i/dac_q ≥ 300 µm with wide rings (−23 dB), or deep n-well under the DAC current sources. Converters: DAC output error per mV of substrate bounce from the extracted netlist. |
| INT23-7 | **Ring and wired blocks next to the ADCs.** pll_cdr ↔ adc_q is 93 µm in v6 (rule exists only for adc_i): +52 / +30 / −5 dB. Wired front end ↔ adc_q 25 µm: +27 / +5 / −23 dB in wired modes. wrx_cap (unplaced): the 200 µm rule passes nominal only. | assembly, spec | pll_cdr ↔ adc_i and adc_q ≥ 300 µm with wide rings. wrx_cap, ctle, detect ↔ ADC ≥ 500 µm with wide rings (or 420 µm in a tie sea), unless the spec states that no converter runs in a wired mode (then no demand). |
| INT23-8 | **lo_mmd next to core_bias (76 µm) and the LNA (142 µm).** Transfer −3…−9 dB to core_bias: 0.7 / 2.0 / 13.8 mV at 2.4 GHz on the bandgap substrate (−3 dB against 1 mV on VBG at the pessimistic bound). LNA passes (+7 dB). | assembly, core_support | lo_mmd ↔ core_bias bandgap core ≥ 300 µm, or the bandgap at the far end of the core_bias slice. core_support: bandgap output against a 2.4 GHz substrate tone (rectification). |
| INT23-9 | **Tie density and backside contact are the two biggest levers.** A1 assumed every free cell is tied; with only block footprints tied the same pairs are 5–40 dB worse. A grounded backside adds 20–45 dB at ≥ 0.5 mm. | assembly, board, spec | Assembly: fill every free area ≥ 50 µm wide between a switching block and an analog block with p+ taps strapped to that analog block's ground (state the coverage in the v7 report). Board: SUB-10 conductive die attach stays mandatory; add a bench check of backside-to-VSS resistance on die 1. |
| INT23-10 | **Converter clock choice sets the aliasing.** With an int/ref/aux 40 or 80 MHz converter clock the macro and D2H lines alias to 1.875 / 3.75 / 5.625 / 7.5 / 9.375 MHz. With the host-derived 39.0625 or 78.125 MHz clock they land on DC. In-band RF lines in RF modes: 2400 (40/80 MHz clock), 2421.875 (macro, 78.125/39.0625 clock), 2437.5 (62.5 MHz core), 2440, 2460.9375, 2480. | spec, digital, converters | Spec: RF modes use the host-derived converter clock by default (same as INT1-9); add the spur table of the report, section 8.2, to the channel plan (SP-01); with the 125 MHz host use 62.5 / 2ⁿ MHz. Keep D2H slow slew in RF modes (the carrier-derived D2H clock puts its 32nd harmonic on the LO: −114 dBm worst with slow slew, −104 dBm fast). |
| INT23-11 | **`spec/package-channel.json` west pad order is stale** (RF_TX adjacent to AUX_CLK; `contract.json` `bond_map_current` has BB_Q between them). Its crosstalk table is wrong for the west edge. | pads, integration (M4) | Regenerate the per-pin package model from `bond_map_current` before M4; add pair-to-pair mutual inductance for non-adjacent RF/LO pairs. |
| INT23-12 | **Two rings on one `pt_pll_vreg2`: simulated, passes at tt.** Output-to-output isolation −20…−24 dB at the ring fundamental and −26…−27 dB at 8 f (PD36 estimated −30 dB); ring B leaves 0.10 mV on ring A's supply; lock range ≤ 15 kHz (22 ppm), closed-loop spur ≤ 1.5 ps pk against 5 ps. Each ring's own /8 and /4 divider current adds 1–2 ps pk of self-jitter on the regulated output. | timing, pll_vreg, wired_rx | Record the measured isolation in PD36. One corner pair (ss/32 °C, ff/22 °C) of `scratch/integration/m23-substrate-pulling/m3a/z3.cir` before LO-02 closes. Confirm the stand-alone ring jitter figures were taken on the regulator, not on an ideal supply. |
| INT23-13 | **Forwarded wire clock from AUX_CLK (CP4-3), ~3.1 mm route.** Hand estimate: 0.9 / 4 / 13 ps pk picked up from the macro's ground bounce if the route is single-ended CMOS between two grounds (limit 5 ps). In RF modes the same route with a 40 / 80 MHz clock puts its 60th / 30th harmonic at 2400 MHz under the route. | assembly, timing, wired_rx, digital | Route it as a shielded differential pair from the `rx_aux_clk` output (never from the pad net: that would hang a 0.5 pF stub on the Q LO pad and carry the 2.4 GHz LO across the die), regenerate at the wired island on the wired ground; ≥ 300 µm from the QVCO spirals, tank nets, LNA and RF_RX pads; `rx_aux_clk.EN` = 0 in RF modes unless AUX is the selected converter clock. Extract the route and rerun the CDR jitter with it. |

Negative control (PD = 0): pad floats to 2.6 V with `Y` = 1 at tt/ss, 0.75 nA flips it at ff/85. Setting the strap costs 20-35 uA from a source below ~10 kohm. | PC-5 | **Bias outputs with ten islands (answers D-BIAS, MW-30, USB-D6 and the bank-rail question; proposal, needs power delivery + chip-top).** Facts (extracted, `scratch/pads-core/bias/EVIDENCE.md`): a mirror output whose rail differs from its trunk diode's rail is wrong by 3.4 % per 10 mV, and a trunk whose diode rail is off pulls every mirror gate on it to ground (15x output, always-on audit "TRK_RF -> VSS"). So no bank or tap may sit on a rail other than its diode's, and a bank may not serve consumers whose islands can be on while the bank's rail is off. With ten islands the five banks no longer satisfy that (rf bank feeds rf_rx and rf_tx; pll bank feeds pll, lo and lod consumers; wire bank feeds wired_rx and wired_tx), and `bias_ext` as bound (`RAIL_pll~RAIL_lo`, `RAIL_wired~RAIL_wired_tx` on trunks whose diodes sit on `RAIL_pll` / `RAIL_wired_rx`) would be wrong in both ways. **Proposal: bind all five bank rails (`RAIL_rf/conv/pll/wired/debug` of `pt_core_bias`) and the taps to the always-on core `VDD`.** No layout change (the banks are inside `pt_core_bias`, the rail pins are top-level pins); each output is already gated by its consumer's enable, which per D-EN contains that consumer's `island_on`; `ON_<bank>` = OR of the member islands' `island_on` (or tied high); disabled outputs leak <= 6.5 pA into an off island and every output junction is reverse-biased there. Cost: +10 uA per enabled output on the core rail (<= 0.3 mA) and the core rail's ripple instead of the island's on the mirror (10-26 uA/V at 78 MHz for 0.3-3 pF of trunk capacitance, i.e. 1-2.6 uA of ripple on a 10 uA output per 100 mV; 63-156 mV/V at the consumer's diode node; a 5 pF trunk-to-rail capacitor would cut 78 MHz to 8-11 uA/V and is not built). **New outputs: `pt_bias_tap2`** (two switched 10 uA outputs on an existing trunk, no diode; 93.7 x 23.7 um; staged, review running) used twice: on `TRK_PLL` for `qvco.IBIAS` + `lo_pfdcp.IBIAS`, on `TRK_WIRE` for `hsbias.IBIAS` + the TX-pad detect bias input (`pt_txpad_bias.IBIAS_DET`). `pt_cdr.IBIAS` keeps the ex-`pll_ring_host` output (nobody shares). | power delivery, chip-top, timing/LO, wired TX, assembly | Power delivery: accept the banks on the core rail or name the rail per bank. Chip-top: replace `bias_ext` by two `pt_bias_tap2` instances (pins `VDD VSS TRK EN0 EN1 OUT0 OUT1`), `VDD` = the bank's rail, placed abutting `pt_core_bias`. LO owner: the QVCO bias then comes from a mirror on the core rail, not on `VDD_LO`: state the ripple the QVCO bias pin tolerates (LO20). |

(2) HS-pair signal detect / electrical idle does not belong in the pad: the pad pin is a 2.4 GHz node with a 1.15 pF and return-loss budget, and the CTLE output already has the gain; it should be an envelope comparator after the CTLE in wired RX (owner: wired RX), with the HS pad unchanged. (3) Bandgap at `ena` = 0 draws 71 uA (the core always runs; `ena` only tri-states the output buffer): kept as is. The cell is vendored and silicon-proven, a supply header would change its PSRR and start-up, and 91 uA at reset is 2 % of the 4.62 mA stopped allocation.
